Lossy against stock by definition (it moves toward the exact result) | NOT a switch yet (a switch with no tile would make every arm an inert base): host reference `src/kernels/act_f16_test.cpp` (the dot of random 2-bit-class weights with the activation rounded as Q8_1 does against rounded to fp16, each against float64: mean relative error Q8_1 2.2e-2 / 5.9e-2 / 4.3e-2, fp16 1.1e-3 / 1.2e-3 / 8.5e-4 for gaussian / x20 outliers / heavy tail, i.e. 20x to 50x closer; a fixture, not a model result, T-889) and probe `tools/pancake/probes/act_f16.hip.cpp` (`act_f16_probe`: a 2-bit dequantise-and-multiply-add matvec with fp16 activations and with int8-rounded ones against the read-only roofline of the same bytes; rule in its header: within ~10% of read-only means the arithmetic is hidden and only the quality question is open; compiled for gfx1100, T-889). The real tile (the IQ2_XS grid lookups feeding a packed fp16 dot, `STRATA_ACT_F16=1`) is the large part and is NOT written | BOX: `build-hip/act_f16_probe`; when the tile exists: spec with arms base / `STRATA_ACT_F16=1` (lossy: `quality_gate.py` token forcing against the Q8_0 reference, `task_gate.py`, top-1 vs Q8_0 is the number), then a decode speed A/B | large (kernel) | HOST REFERENCE + PROBE CODED; tile NOT written, no spec until it exists (promotion waits for the tile and a box run) |

The rdna_kq kernel has no arm yet | offline: `pytest tools/pancake/test_ablate_expdec.py` (2 tests, T-883); default path identical: the preprocessed iq_kernels.cu without the macro is token-identical to HEAD's (whitespace only), and with the macro 1 and 2 it passes the same semantic checks as without (the 3 errors the SDK 6.2 clang already gave for `__shared__ alignas`, no new one, so the file does not compile whole on Windows and no digest could be taken; T-883); BOX: `python3 tools/pancake/ablate_expdec.py build --out bench/results/<day>-ablate-expdec` | small | CODED, NOT RUN on the box; no spec (a build-level ablation, not an A/B of two env arms) |

Not leads (read, no value for this box): the persistent kernel's claim / done counter protocol details beyond O7, the two-card tensor-parallel exchange and its micro-chunk overlap, the Qwen3-30B-A3B long-context YaRN mode, the Ember agent environment, the panel and SLSA release signing, the context-note and advisor harness add-ons (agent quality, not speed), and every sampler and tokenizer item. Two-GPU items stay parked here as in section 8 of MOD_PLAN.md.

Owner direction of 2026-11-17 and 2026-11-18 (adapt our code to work the way the authors intended, in our own code, with a test and a box spec; code every lead, test it, promote only where a result exists): all 14 are now coded or probed, none has run on a GPU, so nothing is promoted and every promotion waits for a box run. O1 to O4: tools with tests. O5: `pcie_link.py` + `link_touch_probe`. O6: snapshot kernel + planner + host test, prompt loop not wired (waits for spec 142's price). O7: protocol, launcher, device template, host test, card parity test, decode not wired (the stage kernels need task-number bodies). O8: four-row verify-window kernel, specs 199 / 200. O9: `STRATA_QSA_SPLIT_GRID`, specs 197 / 198. O10: host reference + probe, tile not written. O11 / O12: specs 168 and 167. O13: `predict` / `stop` keys in spec_lint, used by specs 197 to 200. O14: ablation macro + `ablate_expdec.py`. Compiler-side finding of the O1 run (T-859, unconfirmed whether each instance is launched): of 421 kernel instances in 6 hot sources 66 have scratch or VGPR spills (the worst is `gr_down_v3_kernel` at 256 VGPRs with up to 432 spilled, occupancy 5) and 31 sit at 6 waves per SIMD or fewer; `native_mmvq.cu` does not compile on the Windows SDK 6.2 (`__builtin_amdgcn_sdot4` needs a newer clang), and `iq_kernels.cu`, `moe_f16_kq.cu`, `moe_fused_iq.cu` need ggml headers: the box run of `kernel_resources.py` covers them. 2026-11-18: `iq_kernels.cu` also stops on `__shared__ alignas(16)` (3 sites) under SDK 6.2.

## Part P: hipfire (warpfront/hipfire, "RDNA-native LLM inference engine in Rust", master d435a753, pushed 2026-11-17; read 2026-11-17 through the GitHub API only, never cloned, nothing built or run; one file's logic ported under its Apache-2.0 licence, see L1)

What it is: a Rust + HIP engine for AMD GPUs (RDNA1 to RDNA4, CDNA), 818 stars, its own weight formats (MQ2..MQ6 FWHT-rotated, MFP4, E8P-I4), a retained-PM4 replay layer ("Redline"), native MTP and DFlash drafting, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (the same model as ours: 512 experts, top 10, hidden 2560, intermediate 640, 48 layers), Qwen3.8-27B dense and others. Read for this part: README.md, STATE.md (a stale note on an abandoned gfx1100 PM4 branch), REBUDGET.md (an R9700 hot-set rebudget), ROUTE-MIRROR.md (a gfx1201 to gfx942 CDNA port design: no use here), PRIOR-ART.md, CREDITS.md, CHANGELOG.md (v0.4.4 to v0.4.7, every gfx1100 entry), AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md (their testing rules), docs/methodology/lessons-0.4.5.md, perf-benchmarking.md, consumer24-k1-gfx1100.md, QUANTIZATION.md, speculation-support-inventory.md, 30 perf-checkpoints (the gfx1100 verifier, MTP ring, CPU-MoE splice and EP2 records), `crates/hipfire-runtime/src/ngram_mod.rs`, the headers of hipfire:kernels/src/attention_verify_wmma.gfx1100.hip, `attention_verify_gqa.gfx1100.hip` and hipfire:kernels/gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_k2_pm_gfx1100.hip, the autoresearch loop (`autoresearch/ar/{census,gate/perf_policy,certify/verdict}.py`, `config/loop_gfx1201.toml`) and the receipts/ and redline/ listings. Every number below is `[read]`: theirs, on their hardware, never ours.

**Licence, read from LICENSE, NOTICE, CREDITS.md, PRIOR-ART.md and the file headers (GitHub shows NOASSERTION because the repository is dual-layout, not because a licence is missing):**
- The work as a whole is **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE-APACHE`, the outbound licence since v0.3.0; v0.2.1 and earlier stay MIT OR Apache-2.0, tag `v-mit-final` is MIT). Redistributing Apache-licensed parts needs the licence text and a readable `NOTICE` copy (Apache section 4).
- Files whose authors have not opted in stay **MIT** (`LICENSE-MIT`, per-file `SPDX-License-Identifier`; "MIT OR Apache-2.0" where both sides wrote the file). Authors opted into Apache so far: Kaden Schutt and Kevin Read. So the licence is per file: read the SPDX line of a file before taking its code.
- Files read here: `ngram_mod.rs` SPDX Apache-2.0, Kaden Schutt; `attention_verify_wmma.gfx1100.hip` Apache-2.0, Ben1078; `attention_verify_gqa.gfx1100.hip` Apache-2.0, Kaden Schutt.
- `third_party/` differs: `third_party/ggml/` is a verbatim llama.cpp subset (pin 2e0d86be, MIT, kept MIT, not relabelled), `kernels/src/ggml_*.hip` are Apache-2.0 shims over it, `third_party/amd_matrix_instruction_calculator` is a ROCm submodule (licence not read). The XDNA2 NPU crates carry Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception, MIT (aie-rt) and a GPL-2.0-with-syscall-note UAPI re-declaration; none of it is relevant here.
- **What we did:** L1 ports the logic of `MtpCopyContext` (Apache-2.0) to C++; the file header says so, `LICENSES/hipfire-Apache-2.0.txt` carries the licence text and the NOTICE attribution lines, README.md section 9 credits it. L2, L5 and L9 are our own implementations of ideas, no hipfire text in them. Everything else is ideas only.

**Their numbers against ours (caveats first: no case matches).** Ours (MOD_PLAN.md section 1; T-822, first trend point 2026-11-17, spec 115 at confirm): RX 7900 XT 20 GB gfx1100, Ryzen 7 5700G (Zen 3, DDR4 ~33 GB/s, PCIe 3.0), Qwen3.8-Flash-Next IQ2_XS (2.3-bit class pack), daily preset: decode short 106.9 +-7.6, edit 174.3 +-3.7 tok/s; prompt long1k / long3k / long64k 801.2 / 1284.2 / 2405.7 tok/s. Theirs (CHANGELOG.md v0.4.5 / v0.4.6 gfx1100 entries): RX 7900 XTX 24 GB, host CPU not stated, the same model at MQ4-XTS (about 4-bit experts, a 71 GB split base plus a 29 GB PLE sidecar: most experts live in host RAM and are read over PCIe or computed on the CPU), "tier package" on by default, `hipfire bench`, 4 alternating fresh processes x 3 runs, 1K-token prompts: native MTP decode code 62.0 / prose 49.5 tok/s, AR decode 41.3 / 37.5; TTFT 2.15 s (code) / 1.67 s (prose); prefill pp1105 847, pp8192 1,082, pp16384 2,099 tok/s; random-token-id decode at tg128 34.2 (their own caveat: a ranked hot set has nothing to hit there, -7%). Same card, MQ3-XTS (lossy): MTP code 53.7, AR code 38.2 after the pool-spin fix; pp256 481, pp1105 971 tok/s. R9700 (gfx1201, 32 GB), Flash-Next MQ4-XTS split base: AR 49.7 (code) / 45.6 (prose), MTP 82.9 / 48.5, pp16384 6,648 tok/s. Dense Qwen3.8-27B MQ4-XT on the 7900 XTX with native MTP, Q8 KV: 136.5 code 1K, 141.1 prose 1K, 129.5 code 32K, 79.7 prose 32K tok/s (another model, listed for the verify-kernel rows). Their quality row: MQ2-XTS Flash-Next on the XTX, mean KLD against an FP8 reference 0.1966 (WikiText-2 decode) and 0.2083 (code24), accepted over their 6% budget by an owner exception; ours is top-1 agreement (Q2_0 80.6% vs Q8_0, T-377): another metric. Reading: our decode is about 1.7x their MTP code decode on a card with less VRAM, but we run 2-bit weights (4-bit moves twice the bytes) and their host is unstated; the ratio says nothing about engine quality. Their prefill (847 at 1K, 2,099 at 16K) is below ours (801 / 1,284 / 2,406 at 1K / 3K / 64K) only at the short end by little; also not comparable (their experts stream per layer over the host link). Their own cross-engine bar (CHANGELOG v0.4.5, 2x R9700 EP2): code C8 1.026x of another open engine (CI [0.985, 1.052]), story cells 0.72-0.92x: they do not claim a lead there either.

How they measure, in short (their AGENTS.md "Don't claim a perf win without", lessons-0.4.5.md sections 1-4 and 9; our comparison in L9-L12): 4+ fresh-process loads per arm interleaved ABBA or Latin square with a throwaway arm first, 6-12 when the effect is under 1 ms per step; a paired bootstrap 95% CI of the difference (a bar claim quotes the pooled median, the ratio's CI and how many loads cleared it); prompt md5 and binary md5 recorded; a log line proving the lever ran; an A/A control on any oracle; host load checked per run even when the scheduler says clean; the build that lands is the one measured; a ceiling (Amdahl) and the cheapest kill experiment BEFORE a build (their symlab: the opaque routed-expert launch is 85-95% of the modelled step, so no exact rewrite could pay: stopped on the ceiling, not on a run); a counterfactual model (aggregate = N / (TTFT + steps x step + tail)) that must reproduce the measured medians before it is used; isolated kernel wins (NT1 +5-6% in the bench, P3 1.3-3.5x per GEMM) were flat or worse served. Most of this our abtest already does (ABBA, warm-up pass, design effect, quiet gate, base-again floor, T-822 base n=6); the gaps are L9 to L12.

| L3 Verify-row attention with ONE KV scan shared by all query rows of a kv head, and a WMMA split-K variant (hipfire hipfire:kernels/src/attention_verify_gqa.gfx1100.hip and `attention_verify_wmma.gfx1100.hip`, Apache-2.0, Ben1078 PRs #987/#989): a row group of up to 8 rows per wave group, 6 q heads each, Q8 KV, head_dim 256; the WMMA one keeps Q fragments in VGPRs, fills K and V^T planes in alternating phases with LDS-only barriers, merges two key-half streams at the end, fixed grid independent of context `[read]` | dense 27B MQ4, 7900 XTX: multi-row R4/R8 verify attention 33.4 -> 46.3 tok/s (+38.6%, 21.5K context, DFlash, 6 fresh processes, text md5 identical); WMMA split-K stacked on the int8 verify GEMV: code 32K 95.7 -> 121.2 (1.27x), prose 32K 59.0 -> 73.3 (1.24x), 1K neutral | WMMA one numeric (f16), R4/R8 byte-identical | CODED 2026-11-17 AS A DIFFERENT, SMALLER KERNEL (second pass below); NOT their kernel, because the kernels are written for the 27B's 24 q / 4 kv heads, head_dim 256 and their Q8 KV layout, ours is the QSA int8 / q4 KV with other shapes, and there is no GPU here to build or compare a port, so a faithful port would be a blind rewrite. HAVE the nearest thing: `STRATA_ATTN_WMMA` (BitDecoding style decode attention on matrix cores; kernels n_q 1 and 3 x1.71-1.80 / x1.45-1.59 in the kernel bench, T-023, T-031; end to end neutral at 36K, T-019, T-032; token forcing PASS T-300). NOT in ours: a kernel for 4-16 verify rows sharing one scan (our ECAP windows are 9+ rows, the MTP window 5). The attention share of our window is small (MOD_PLAN.md), but rule 8: switch to add `STRATA_ATTN_WMMA_ROWS=8` (extend `qsa_decode_attn_wmma.cu` from 3 to 8 rows). Test: spec on `longdec32k` / `longdec64k`, screen then confirm, token forcing. Effort: large. SECOND PASS 2026-11-17: read our verify path before porting. Our verify rows do not share a KV scan: QSA selects each row's own top-k cells (`sel_` per row, `verify.cpp`), only a dense / identity selection (the drafter, short contexts) is the same for every row, and the one-row WMMA kernel already fills M with the 12 heads of a kv head, so hipfire's shared-K-scan layout has nothing to share here and a faithful port would need all R rows' Q fragments in VGPRs (64 VGPRs a row: R = 4 is 256, the whole file). What IS coded: `STRATA_ATTN_WMMA_ROWS=2 / 4 / 8` (with `STRATA_ATTN_WMMA=1`; `decode_attn_wmma_rows_kernel`, `qsa_decode_attn_wmma.cu`): up to R rows of one `qsa_decode_attn_batch` call per block; per row the same instruction sequence as the one-row kernel, so bit-identical outputs are expected; the staged V rows and scales are read once per group when the rows' selections are identical for the chunk (same width, same cells), else restaged. Honest scope: it saves launches and V staging, not K traffic; it can lose (fewer blocks per launch, and the int8 rows kernel spills 49 VGPRs against 0, T-878). The parity harness `hip_decode_attn_wmma_parity` now compares 2 / 4 / 8 rows with the one-row kernel byte for byte (and two shared-selection cases); CODED, UNTESTED on a GPU, `conversation_disk_env.def` Speed. Specs 192 (screen + confirm, judged against `wmma`, not `base`) and 193 (token forcing). Promotion waits for box run; nothing to promote without a result. STOP POINT: hipfire's WMMA split-K kernel itself (Q fragments in VGPRs, two key-half streams) is not adapted: our rows have different cells, so the stop is a data fact, not a lack of time; revisit only if a verify-window trace shows identical selections across rows. Licence: Apache-2.0 files; if code is ever taken keep the SPDX header, add Ben1078 and Kaden Schutt to README section 9 |
| L4 Lazy online-softmax rescale in the f16 WMMA attention (`attention_verify_wmma.gfx1100.hip` `VW_THRESH`): the running max moves only when a tile exceeds it by more than 8 (log2 units), so P stays at most 2^8 (f16-safe) and the accumulator rescale is skipped on most tiles; the algebra is exact, only rounding changes `[read]` | no number for this part alone | numeric (rounding only) | our online-softmax kernels (`qsa_prompt_attn.cu` base-2 rescale, `qsa_decode_attn_wmma.cu`) rescale on every max change. Effort: small per kernel, blind without the box. CODED 2026-11-17 (second pass below). SECOND PASS 2026-11-17: our decode WMMA kernel has no online softmax (per-chunk max, a merge kernel), so the rescale lives only in the two gfx11 prompt kernels (`prompt_attn_wmma_kernel`, `prompt_attn_wmma_q4_kernel`, `qsa_prompt_attn.cu`). Coded as the build macro `STRATA_ATTN_LAZY_RESCALE=T` (T = 1..8 log2 units, default 0): the running maximum moves only when a chunk exceeds it by more than T, alpha is exactly 1 otherwise, the V scale headroom shrinks by 2^T (`STRATA_PA_VTOP`) so p * vs * vup stays inside FP16 (hipfire's f16 range works without that scale; ours would overflow above T = 1 without it), and a wave whose 8 alphas are all 1 skips the multiply. The skip has a price: T-879 the TR=0 int8 kernels spill 66 / 90 / 98 VGPRs (default 0 / 10 / 13), so `STRATA_ATTN_LAZY_NOSKIP` (the lazy maximum only, resources identical to the default) is the variant to try if the skip loses. The default build's ISA is identical to HEAD's. Specs 194 (arms lazy2, lazy2-noskip, lazy8) and 195 (token forcing). CODED, UNTESTED; promotion waits for box run. Ceiling still unmeasured: `hip_decode_attn_wmma_parity --bench` does not cover the prompt kernels; the prompt spec is the measurement. Licence: idea only |

Their weights are MQ4, ours IQ2_XS: the idea (share X across waves in LDS) maps to our IQ2_XS dequant -> WMMA f16 path, the kernel does not. Switch: `STRATA_PF_MOE_XLDS=1` once a kernel exists. Test: spec on `long3k` / `long64k`. Effort: large. CODED 2026-11-17 as a build macro (second pass below). SECOND PASS 2026-11-17: read our F16 prompt-expert kernels first: `native_f16_kernel` (`moe_fused_iq.cu`, the IQ2 / IQ3 / IQ4 formats) and `gate_up_kernel` / `down_kernel` (`moe_f16_kq.cu`, K-quants) already stage the activation tile once per work item into LDS through `ActStage` and all 8 waves read their B fragments from it, with a register prefetch of the next stage. So the 'share X across waves' half of the lead is HAVE; what differs is hipfire's double-buffered LDS (one barrier per K64) against our single buffer (two barriers per stage). Coded: build macro `STRATA_PF_MOE_XLDS` in `native_f16_kernel` (a second stage buffer, one barrier per stage, same arithmetic; the 128-row tile keeps one buffer because 2 x 34 KB exceeds the 64 KB limit; T-880: LDS up 13.5-40.6 -> 26.9-51.7 KB, waves/SIMD 6-16 -> 4-8). The K-quant kernels are not changed. Spec 194 arm `xlds`. CODED, UNTESTED; promotion waits for box run (occupancy may eat the barrier saving). Licence: Apache-2.0 per header (read to line 22 only); ideas only |
| L15 Quant-method ideas (hipfire QUANTIZATION.md, PRIOR-ART.md, hipfire:docs/MQ_I4_A4_GFX1100_G1_ORACLE.md, hipfire:docs/consumer24-k1-gfx1100.md): FWHT rotation of every weight group before 3-4 bit quantisation (activations rotated by a fused rmsnorm kernel), per-group AWQ alpha refit ("XTS"), trunk and head at 6-bit with experts at 4-bit, a 132-byte block with sub-block scales that beat an affine layout at equal bytes, and a TWO-BUCKET expert layer: hot experts MQ4, cold experts in a 2-bit E8 lattice codec (E8P-I4) in one indexed route `[read]` | MQ2-XTS Flash-Next on the XTX: KLD 0.1966 / 0.2083 vs FP8 (wt2 / code24 decode); dense MQ4 KLD wt2 0.042 / code24 0.030 on the pinned fixture | lossy | OURS: the daily packs come from outside (ISTA-DASLab -> Unsloth -> Gyro, AGENTS.md), GGUF per-tensor types: no per-expert mix inside a layer, no FWHT. There is no path from their `.mq2-xts` to our GGUF reader, so the only test is offline: quantise one layer with an FWHT-rotated 2-bit codec and compare weight MSE / output KLD against IQ2_XS at equal bytes, using the profile's expert counts for the hot / cold split. Switch: none (a model file). Effort: very large; needs their CPU-only quantiser (Apache-2.0 `hipfire-quantize`) and the box for any speed claim. Parked as research; the checked second pass is below. SECOND PASS 2026-11-17 (research note only, no switch: a quantiser is a model file, the engine has nothing to flip): the offline half that CAN run is already coded: `tools/pancake/probes/rotation_probe.py --wbits 2,3,4` (Part L, T-851 / T-852) measures the weight and product error of a block-Hadamard-rotated round-to-nearest quantiser against the unrotated one on tensors exported from the box, and `tools/pancake/rotate_gptq.py` is the GPTQ variant. What it does not do: the IQ2_XS codebook, hipfire's per-group AWQ alpha refit, the E8P lattice codec, or the hot / cold two-bucket layer; the last needs the profile's expert counts (`bench/` profiles) and a second codec that does not exist here. Recorded so nobody re-reads QUANTIZATION.md for a switch: there is none. Licence: Apache-2.0 per file; ideas only |

Already in our tree, so no row: the MTP draft-vocabulary subset (their #998, 65,536 tokens, +7.4-9.4% decode on top of the other two: ours is `data/draft_vocab*.bin`, English 40.5K best, full and zh60k flat or worse, T-109, T-121, T-123, T-146); int8-activation GEMV for 2-5 verify rows (#987: our verify GEMVs are the ggml MMVQ q8_1 path and `STRATA_MMVF_ROWS1`); MTP checkpoint ring and window repair after EOS or a tool call (their 126 s -> 29 s at 135K tokens, a 19,729-token rewind 50.7 s -> 10.6 s: ours is `conversation_cache.hpp` strict-prefix reuse, `CKPT_*`, T-575 family); `HIPFIRE_HIP_WAIT=spin` (rejected there; ours sets `cudaDeviceScheduleSpin`, generate.cpp:4724); the CPU-splice facts (int8 activations a null because the splice streams weights at ~26 GB/s of the box's 46-55 GB/s, a batched multi-expert GEMV region +47%, prefix-broadcast 3/6-bit decode +4.7%, adaptive rayon grain flat: our pool is C++ with `EXPDEC` and the hugetlb copy; the bandwidth probe `tools/pancake/probes/expert_bw.py` was never run, MOD_PLAN.md 4.2.3); MTP depth priced only from measured per-arch costs (lessons section 5: ours `DraftPolicy::cost_ms` plus the union fit `STRATA_DRAFT_UFAC` for unmeasured widths, T-762); Qwen3.5/3.6 dense items, EP2 / TP expert and tensor parallelism, the NPU, vision, image generation and the gfx942 route mirror (no second GPU and no CDNA here: parked, hardware this box does not have).

## Part Q: Strata-HotRod (sangharshadhyeta/Strata-HotRod, MIT, main 9d3b6c305 of 2026-11-20, 53 stars; cloned 2026-11-20 to a directory outside our repo, read only, nothing built or run)

What it is: a fork of upstream Strata for a LOW-RAM, SSD-STREAMING box: RTX 3060 12 GB, i5-12400F, 30 GB RAM, PCIe 3.0 NVMe, Rocky Linux 9.6, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next IQ3_S (78 GB; the same model family as ours, a different quant). Fork point: upstream v0.1.45 (merge-base 477ac13cb; tags `v0.1.45-hotrod.N`); 473 own commits dated 2026-10-10 to 2026-11-20 (about 330 by "root" = the owner's agent, 24 by sangharshadhyeta, many co-authored with Claude Opus 5.5), 254 files, +45,351 / -2,324 lines: src +18.7K (verify.cpp, native_router.cu, expert_source.cpp, generate.cpp, prefill.cpp, mtp.cpp, subfit_*), tools +11.8K, include +3.6K, docs +3.3K, tests +2.6K, serve +2.5K (web UI, prompt cache, Monitor timeline), SPEC.md +1.3K lines. It is 5,392 commits BEHIND upstream v0.2.16 (it merged upstream 0.1.45 on 2026-10-17 and hand-ported a few upstream fixes on 2026-11-07 and 2026-11-13). Side branches: `upstream-dsv41` (DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash on upstream 0.2.7.1, 2,378 ahead), `hotrod-edge0` (Edge0-145B NVFP4 latent MoE, 17 ahead), `audit`, `recheck-gated`, `standin-samples`, `living-model`, `draft-cold`, `dflash-port`, `glm53-flash`, `hotrod-site`. A second repo, AAO-IS2/HotRod (created 2026-11-18, GitHub shows "Other": the MIT text with "Strata-HotRod contributors", 0 stars), is only a write-up page, a PDF note and a copy of the changed files (hotrod-qwen/, hotrod-deepseek/, solid-cpp/): no code beyond the main repo.

Licence and credit: MIT ("Copyright (c) 2026 Niko1221 and the Strata contributors; Copyright (c) 2026 Sangharsh Adhyeta and Strata-HotRod contributors"). Code may be used with credit in README.md section 9 and the notice. README.md section 9 ALREADY credits sangharshadhyeta (Strata-HotRod) for the stand-in system, routing budget, fragile re-check and the substitute fit (`a2ef1638cf`, `870e32b4b3` ... `850ddf0781`). Adopting any lead below needs ONE more credit line naming the sha: Q1 `d0aa072f6` (read while typing), Q2 `fd41f7174` (PROMPT_TAIL), Q3 `cb59a8130` / `5e13e762c` (MTP slots), Q5 `890990bbb` (MTP_KV_FP16). README is not edited by this part. Nothing of theirs is copied: every row is read from their commits and docs as untrusted data.

Their numbers against ours (caveats first: no case matches). Theirs: decode 17.2 (stock Strata, SSD-bound) to 50-70 tok/s on short prompts, RTX 3060; their decode window is bound by NVMe reads (3.3 GB/s ceiling) and by the CPU pool; the audit branch's own stage times (ms/window): router 12.8, cpu-wait 14.2, pcie-experts 9.0, vram-experts 7.8, gdn 7.4. Ours: RX 7900 XT, arena fully in RAM, decode short about 107-118, edit about 160-174 tok/s (T-822, T-628 base), `--pcie-frac 0`. So almost everything they win is SSD / page-cache / PCIe work that we do not have (this file, "SSD/page-cache-tier leads do not apply"): layer-major O_DIRECT prompt reads (45K prompt 119 s to 67 s), the RAM tier and its give-back, `IO_PF_THREADS`, the read-ahead gate, page-cache warm, `PIN_SEGS` (+9% together with `PCIE_BALANCE`, a lever our `--pcie-frac 0` result removes, B16), the learned look-ahead as a READ predictor (91/85/78% recall at 1/2/4 layers), the DS4 ring. These get no row. Their own audit (branch `audit`, docs/AUDIT-functions.md, 8 Nov, 413 requests) is the useful part: each learner they switched on COSTS decode on their box (feature off gives: re-check +25%, GPU look-ahead +8.7%, tail read +5.8%, learned look-ahead +5.5%, stand-in learn + mask +2.9%), and several of their tuners "never ran" or "decide on noise".

Already ours, so no row: the stand-in system, routing budget, auto budget, fragile re-check with brake and K0, prompt stand-ins and the substitute fit (all coded 2026-11-16, README census; the family is REJECTed by the quality gate T-961, the substitute fit DROPPED T-632); decayed-use swap victim (`tier`); conversation working set (`STRATA_CONV_WORKSET`, include/strata/program/expert_seed.hpp:13); KV streaming past 64K; prompt cache on disk and the turn checkpoint; ElXreno's trained look-ahead predictor (`STRATA_LOOKAHEAD_WEIGHTS`); the DFlash 2 drafter (they parked it: "no faster than the MTP here", `0cf8d746e`; ours closed, see DFlash2 rows).

Quality evidence they cite for their own stack: MMLU-Pro 87.1% (183 of 210, thinking on, 32K, stand-ins on, IQ3_S); 50 maths + 30 coding questions 47 / 50 and 30 / 30 with stand-ins vs 48 / 50 and 30 / 30 without (hand read, too small to show a 1-2 answer difference). Their "50-70 tok/s" headline is their hardware: do not compare it with ours.

Priority order for the rule-8 port of the leads above, highest expected gain on gfx1100 first (all default-off; none ported or tested here; the expectations are `[derived]`, small, and honest about their box being SSD-bound): 1. Q1 read-while-typing warm prefill (exact, user-visible TTFT in the chat UI); 2. Q2 `PROMPT_TAIL` (exact, after-long-prompt decode); 3. Q5 `MTP_KV_FP16` (exact, acceptance on long context); 4. Q3 `MTP_SLOTS` / `MTP_ZC` (exact, 338 MiB to the cache, likely noise); 5. Q7 paired sequential tuner decision (method; no tok/s itself); 6. Q4 `SWAP_GRACE` / `COUNT_KEPT` (exact, under 1%); 7. Q6 `RECHECK_GATE` and 8. Q8 `SHIFT_CAP` (lossy guards, only for a stand-in retry); 9. Q10 `MASK` (lossy, never shown to help); 10. Q11 router fusion / `GR_DOWN_WPC` (exact, measured no gain); then Q12 and Q13 (parked / negative by their own numbers).

## Part R: EXL3 / QTIP (turboderp-org/exllamav3, MIT, dev head 8c593e4e of 2026-11-13, tag v1.6.7; QTIP = Cornell-RelaxML/qtip, GPL-3.0, paper arXiv 2406.11235, CC BY 4.0; read 2026-11-20; the exllamav3 clone was treated as untrusted data in a directory outside our repo, only read, nothing built, run or imported; no code copied)

Study only, owner order "also look at EXL3". Not ported, not tested, no engine work. Tags: `[code]` read in the source, `[measured]` the AUTHOR's number on THEIR hardware (never ours), `[paper]` from the paper or a model card, `[derived]` my reading, `[unverified]` not checked or only second-hand. "Ours" = present / partly / missing, with the file.

Sources. exllamav3: owner turboderp, MIT (Copyright (c) 2025 Turboderp), head v1.6.7 of 13 Nov 2026; the README calls EXL3 "a streamlined variant of QTIP" (star count `[unverified]`, not read). QTIP code: GPL-3.0, 201 stars (GitHub page through the fetch tool, `[unverified]` summary); the paper (Tseng, Sun, Hou, De Sa, NeurIPS 2024 spotlight, v4 of 18 Jun 2025) is CC BY 4.0 (abstract page only; the paper body was NOT read). Public EXL3 quants of OUR model exist: huggingface turboderp/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-exl3 (card licence qwen-community-1.0, 174 likes, created 2026-08-31; raw card read by curl, branch file sizes by the HF tree API). Other EXL3 quants of the family by turboderp (names and dates from the HF API, sizes not read): Qwen3.5-35B-A3B, Qwen3-30B-A3B, Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B (Instruct, Thinking), Qwen3.5-122B-A10B, Qwen3.8-27B.

Already in our tree (do not repeat). (1) `src/dsv41/vendor/exl3.cu`, `src/dsv41/vendor/exl3_legacy.cu`, `src/dsv41/vendor/exl3_cpu.cpp`, `include/strata/dsv41/exl3.hpp`: EXL3 kernels vendored from Project Maya v1.1.17 (MIT, mw00), following exllamav3's codebooks, trellis window and tensor-core fragment decode; used by the DeepSeek V4.1 path only (ds4_model.cu); CUDA mma on sm_80+, "F32 FMAs on CUDA cores" on HIP and before sm_80 `[code]`. The format description in the exl3.hpp header matches what I read in exllamav3 `[code]`. (2) A trellis + Hadamard family for Qwen3.8 experts is already in the tree as Gyro (agentionai TQ2_T / TQK6 / TQK7 / pair trellis): `docs/GYRO.md`, `src/kernels/cpu/tq_computed.hpp`, `src/kernels/cpu/hadamard.cpp`, `tools/dsv41_lab/tqk_lab.cu`, `tools/iq_pack.py`; its decode kernels pass parity on gfx1151 per GYRO.md; we have no Gyro row in TEST_LEDGER.md and no gfx1100 number. (3) Earlier decisions: the README papers table rejects QTIP and QuIP# as "not worth a requant vs IQ2_KT"; LEADS.md Part D has the row "QTIP / IQ*_KT ~0.2 bpw gain (ik)"; MOD_PLAN.md section 8 parks "nasone32 EXL3 (format not portable; its HIP kernel patterns are a lead)"; MOD_PLAN.md excludes ExLlamaV2 as a base engine. Those rejected a requant we would do ourselves. NEW here: a public, calibrated, KLD-measured EXL3 of exactly our model exists at 1.66 to 4.18 bpw, so nothing needs requantising to try it. (4) A fork already runs EXL3 experts for Qwen3.8-Flash-Next on a 7900 XTX: android-imdad/Strata-7900xtx, branch rx7900xtx, MIT, 3 stars; I read only its README (fetch tool and the copy under data/fork-kb); its kernels and its pack tool were NOT read, so the code is `[unverified]` and the numbers are the author's `[measured]`: routed experts of the 2.68bpw_h6_ng8 branch replace IQ3_S experts (50.3 GB to 39.5 GB, 3.33 to 2.61 bits a weight with scales, 8,796 to 11,150 experts in a 24 GB card's cache); against the fork's own IQ3_S experts on the RX 7900 XTX: +9 to 12% decode, +13 to 30% prompt reading; against upstream v0.2.4.1: short chat 87.3 to 115.1 tok/s, 4K prompt 1,066 to 1,882 tok/s; perplexity chat 1.4187 (IQ3_S) vs 1.4218 (EXL3, 0.22% worse), long code and documents 4.4438 vs 4.2122 (5.2% better); the CPU expert path needs AVX-512 and a parity build, and only the experts are swapped (attention, shared expert, router, n-gram table stay IQ3_S). That fork was not in LEADS.md before (only its FK ideas were).

How EXL3 works, at the level we can use `[code]` (exllamav3 at 8c593e4e):
1. A weight (k inputs, n outputs) is stored as int16 trellis [k/16][n/16][16 K]: a 16x16 tile = 256 weights = 256 steps of K bits; K is bits per weight, 1 to 8 (half-integer K such as 1.5 and 2.5 only with the mul1 codebook, tile 16 K + 8 words).
2. Tail-biting bitshift trellis: the state is a 16-bit window sliding K bits at a time over the tile's 256 K bits, wrapping round. No stored codebook: the value of a state is COMPUTED. Three codebooks: 3inst (x*89226354+64248484), mcg (x*0xCBAC1FED), mul1 (x*0x83DCD12D, byte sum by one dp4a, one fp16 FMA); mul1 is the default and is required by the int8 GEMV, the CPU expert offload and half-integer K. Per weight: a funnel shift for the window, one 32-bit multiply, a dp4a, an fp16 FMA (mcg and 3inst add a lop3).
3. Incoherence processing: suh [k] and svh [n] fp16 per-channel scales and signs plus a 128-point Sylvester Hadamard over every 128 consecutive values: y = H(H(x * suh) W') * svh. The weights are rotated once at conversion; the input and output rotations run PER TOKEN on the activations inside the kernels (a warp-shuffle transform). Nothing is un-rotated at load for decode; the prompt path reconstructs fp16 W' tile by tile.
4. CUDA decode GEMV (m <= 8): cooperative kernel, warps split k, B streams to registers (ld.global.cs), the window words resolved by lane shuffles or shared memory, one mma.m16n8k16 pair per 16x16 tile with fp16 accumulate folded to fp32, both Hadamards inside the same cooperative launch (grid.sync twice); the structure is QTIP's inference kernel on the unmodified EXL3 format. Larger m uses a tile GEMM (sm_80+ native; the Turing path emulates k16 with two k8 mma and no cp.async).
5. ROCm / RDNA is an in-repo path (USE_ROCM, a rocm/ tree, 6,131 lines under rocm/quant): "RDNA 3.5 GEMV kernel, fdot2 dot-product form", explicitly NOT a port of the mma kernel: one wave per 16-wide output tile, 16 active lanes each hold one output in fp32, the quantised tile is staged through LDS, decoded in registers, unswizzled to row-major in LDS (stride 18 halves against bank conflicts) and consumed by V_DOT2_F32_F16; bits 1-8, all three codebooks; m = 1 there, m = 2..8 in a multirow kernel, plus an expert-batched multi-matrix GEMV and a fused MoE kernel; a WMMA GEMM with a tuning table for gfx1151 and untuned library shapes for gfx1100/gfx1101; the Hadamards are separate launches; graphs are off by default on ROCm. The env table says the RDNA fdot2 GEMVs beat the int8 GEMV there. No tok/s for gfx1100 in the files I read `[unverified]`.
6. MoE: block-sparse expert kernels (cooperative variants, per-expert K for gate/up/down), routing kernels, expert parallelism and CPU offload of experts (AVX2 / AVX-512, mul1 only). The README architecture list includes Qwen3 MoE, Qwen3-Next, Qwen3.5 MoE and Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (class Qwen4ExpForConditionalGeneration, with the n-gram / PLE table, MTP, vision; a gated delta net kernel gdn.cu is in the tree).
7. Quantiser: HF bf16 in, Hessians on the fly, LDLQ-style rounding with a fused Viterbi kernel on the GPU. convert.py options: -b bits, -hq (raise attention and shared-expert layers, +0.05 to 0.10 bpw on MoE), -mae per-expert Hessians weighted by routing, -rcp per-tensor recipe, -cd own calibration trace, separate head / n-gram / embedding / MTP / vision bits; the working directory must hold a full copy of the output. Cost per the README: "a couple of minutes for smaller models, up to a few hours for larger ones (70B+) on a single high-end consumer GPU"; for a 125B MoE with a 51B PLE table I found no figure `[unverified]`.
8. Optimised ("SC", self-calibrated) quants, doc/optimize.md: sc_trace.py (self-sampled trace), sc_measure.py (per-tensor sensitivity: LDLQ-shaped noise injected into the unquantised model, KLD of the suffix), sc_optimize.py (kld(t,K) = S_t * rfn_t(K)^alpha, exact greedy allocation) giving a recipe.yaml, then convert.py -rcp. The doc calls it experimental and "currently untested on sparse models" `[paper]`; the Qwen3.8 card nevertheless ships SC branches.

Quality and speed claims for Qwen3.8-Flash-Next, the author's (evalscope, single run each, "very questionable" in his own words; GGUF from llama.cpp serve, EXL3 from TabbyAPI; checked against the raw card) `[measured]`:

| quant | bpw | mean KLD | GPQA-D | ERQA | SWE-bench mini |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| EXL3 SC 1.66 | 1.66 | 0.1205 | 86.4% | 64.3% | 74% |
| EXL3 SC 2.18 | 2.18 | 0.0497 | 89.4% | 68.0% | 76% |
| EXL3 SC 2.68 | 2.68 | 0.0249 | 91.4% | 65.5% | 76% |
| EXL3 SC 3.18 | 3.18 | 0.0138 | 91.4% | 68.0% | 76% |
| GSQ-RCO IQ2_XS (our pack's family) | 2.40 | 0.1172 | 87.4% (ISTA-DASLab) | 61.3% | - |
| GSQ-RCO IQ3_XXS | 2.90 | 0.0529 | 91.4% (ISTA-DASLab) | - | - |
| UD3.1 IQ1_S / IQ1_M | 2.31 / 2.42 | 0.0671 / 0.0618 | - | 65.5% / - | 74% / - |
| original | 16 | - | 91.7% | 68.8% | 76% |

Reading `[derived]`: EXL3 1.66 matches the IQ2_XS KLD with 31% fewer bits; EXL3 2.18 beats IQ3_XXS (2.90) on KLD with 25% fewer bits. Caveats: the bpw bases differ (EXL3 counts head, embedding and n-gram settings its own way, a GGUF bpw is the whole file), KLD comes from separate harnesses and traces, the GGUF GPQA figures are another lab's, one run each, the UD3.1 rows are not our pack. Branch sizes (HF tree API, GB; the n-gram table is a separate swappable file): 1.66bpw_h4_ng3 = 30.2 + 19.8 n-gram; 2.18bpw_h6_ng8 = 38.7 + 51.8; 2.68bpw_h6_ng8 = 46.5 + 51.8; 3.18bpw_h6_ng8 = 54.5 + 51.8. Our IQ2_XS pack is 39.2 GB RAM+VRAM (docs/MODELS.md): EXL3 2.18 has about the same body size at 0.42x the author's KLD, EXL3 1.66 is 23% smaller at about the same KLD `[derived]`; the PLE table needs its own comparison.
Speed claims: QTIP README `[paper]`: bs=1, RTX 6000 Ada, 2-bit Llama-2-7B 188 tok/s (QuIP# 186, AQLM 81.5, fp16 55.9). The exllamav3 README and doc/ carry no 3060 / 3090 / 4090 tok/s table, only kernel notes (3090-tuned heuristics, a 4090 + 3090 split) `[unverified]`; the 1.66 card note says "200+ t/s in agentic workloads" on one headless RTX 5090, 64k context `[measured]`. For gfx1100 only the 7900 XTX fork above (its own decode engine, README only).

Fit for us `[derived]` unless tagged.
(a) Quality: the only evidence for our family is the author's card (promising: same KLD at 1.66, about half the KLD at 2.18 for similar bytes); it is not a like-for-like of OUR pack. The measurement we would trust is our own pass on the same text (docs/PERPLEXITY.md method, KLD against the IQ3_S or BF16 run) once a pack exists; none is done.
(b) Speed gfx1100: our decode is limited by PCIe and CPU for experts outside the cache, so fewer bytes per expert matters more than ALU: against our IQ2_XS about -9% expert bytes at 2.18 and about -30% at 1.66; the 7900 XTX fork measured +9 to 12% decode for -22% expert bytes. The decode ALU on RDNA3 is real (32-bit multiply, dot4, funnel shift, fp16 FMA per weight; v_mul_lo_u32 is a quarter-rate instruction `[unverified]`) against our MMVQ's table lookups, so a cache-resident expert may be no faster than IQ2_XS; no one measured that on gfx1100. RDNA3 has WMMA but not the m16n8 shape; exllamav3's own RDNA kernels avoid WMMA at m = 1 and use fdot2 (wave32). Expected value: quality-positive, speed between slightly negative and +10% depending on how many experts fit.
(b) Speed 3060 (sm_86, 12 GB, 30 GB RAM, SSD): the vendored kernels use the sm_80 mma path and exllamav3 treats sm_80+ as native (Turing emulated), so sm_86 is a best-supported case. The large effect is footprint: Strata-HotRod streams a 78 GB IQ3_S from NVMe (stock 17.2 tok/s, theirs 50-70, Part Q `[measured]`), while the EXL3 1.66 body is 30.2 GB (plus a 19.8 GB n-gram table read row by row) against 42 GB of RAM+VRAM, so the experts would stop streaming; our own IQ2_XS (39.2 GB) nearly fits too, so the gain over IQ2_XS is smaller than over IQ3_S. The CPU expert path of exllamav3 and the fork needs AVX-512; the i5-12400F has none (from the CPU model, not checked on the box) `[unverified]`.
(c) Adding an EXL3 weight path to Strata (not done): a pack step reading the safetensors (trellis, suh, svh, mcg / mul1 flags per tensor; 3 tensors per expert; K may differ for gate/up and down) either as an option of `tools/iq_pack.py` or a new tool; an expert type with its byte size in the expert cache and loader (`src/core/expert_source.cpp`, the native expert code); decode expert kernels on HIP and CUDA (adapt moe_gate_up / moe_down of `src/dsv41/vendor/exl3.cu` and exllamav3's RDNA fdot2 pattern; our Gyro kernels are the nearest in-tree precedent); a CPU kernel (`src/dsv41/vendor/exl3_cpu.cpp` exists, AVX-512); a prompt path (reconstruct to fp16 then our GEMM, or decode into tiles as the fork does); dense layers only if attention and the shared expert also move (otherwise they stay as now); parity tests against exllamav3's reconstruct (needs a reference run we cannot make here) and a verifier. The router and the PLE table are unaffected. Rough effort `[derived]`: 2 to 3 weeks focused for experts only, mostly kernels, cache accounting and tests; dense layers and our own quantiser would add several weeks and are not recommended.
(d) Licence and credit: exllamav3 is MIT: code may be used with credit in README.md section 9 and a notice. QTIP code is GPL-3.0: copy none of it (exllamav3 has its own MIT implementation). The QTIP paper is CC BY 4.0. The EXL3 weights of Qwen3.8-Flash-Next carry qwen-community-1.0. Credit line when code is first used: turboderp-org/exllamav3, MIT, commit 8c593e4e5c88da76a4aaf8f1b1838bb7c1fce4e0 (v1.6.7, 2026-11-13), author turboderp. README.md section 9 does not yet name turboderp or mw00's EXL3 kernels (it names mw00 for the loop guard); not edited now (owner order).

Leads. Weights change the model, so all are LOSSY (a new quantisation; quality gate needed) unless marked method.

| lead | evidence | ours | default-off switch and test later (rule 8), expected value |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| XL1 A Qwen3.8 expert pack from turboderp's 2.18bpw_h6_ng8 (and 1.66) experts: pack tool + expert type in the cache + decode kernels; KLD / perplexity and decode on our box against the IQ2_XS pack | author KLD 0.0497 at 2.18, 0.1205 at 1.66, IQ2_XS 0.1172 `[measured]`; fork +9-12% decode on a 7900 XTX for IQ3_S to 2.68 `[measured]`, README only `[unverified]` | MISSING for Qwen3.8 (only the DeepSeek path has it) | pack flag / expert-format switch, off; first an engine-free KLD on our text, then ledger decode arms. gfx1100: quality large, speed -3 to +10% `[derived]`; 3060: footprint large (30 GB body in 42 GB) |
| XL2 Reuse the vendored kernels: move moe_gate_up / moe_down of `src/dsv41/vendor/exl3.cu` into the Qwen3.8 expert path (HIP FMA path exists, CUDA mma on sm_86) and add the exllamav3 RDNA fdot2 GEMV as the gfx1100 decode kernel `[code]` | exllamav3 rocm/quant 6,131 lines, bits 1-8, three codebooks `[code]` | partly (Maya build, DeepSeek only) | kernel-choice switch off; a parity test against the CPU kernel is possible on this box; sets the speed of XL1 |
| XL3 Per-expert Hessians weighted by routing (-mae, -mar) and -hq for attention and shared experts | convert.md, no numbers `[paper]` | the GSQ-RCO / RCO quantiser is ISTA-DASLab's and not in this tree (we only consume the GGUF) | method for any future pack; no switch; value unknown |
| XL4 KLD-driven per-tensor bit allocation (sc_trace, sc_measure, sc_optimize, recipe) as a model for mixed-bit packs of our own | doc/optimize.md: sensitivity, exact greedy allocation, the U-shape flattens on the model's own traces; "untested on sparse models" `[paper]` | missing (`tools/iq_pack.py` repacks, it does not allocate bits) | method; first test is offline: which tensors of our IQ2_XS are most sensitive (needs a GPU run elsewhere) |
| XL5 Hadamard-rotated activations as an error reducer (rotate x per token, quantise the rotated weights) | QuIP# / QTIP incoherence processing, EXL3 step 3 `[code]`; Part D QuaRot row "if rotated GGUF appears" | partly (`src/kernels/cpu/hadamard.cpp` and Gyro prove the plumbing; `src/kernels/cpu/expert_layout.cpp` has a hadamard path) | needs a requant, so only through XL1; no separate switch |
| XL6 Half-integer K (1.5, 2.5 bits) with mul1: finer bit steps than GGUF types | tile of 16 K + 8 words; branches 1.66 and 2.18 `[code]` | missing | part of XL1; whether the vendored kernels handle K = 1.5 is `[unverified]` |
| XL7 The n-gram (PLE) table at 3 bits (19.8 GB) rather than 8 (51.8 GB), swappable between quants | card: the n-gram file is swappable, "usually streamed from disk" `[measured]` | our PLE table stays on the SSD (docs/GYRO.md: about half of each file) | quality of a 3-bit table on its own is not shown (the card folds it into the ng3 1.66 row); disk and page-cache pressure on 30 GB RAM; needs only the PLE row-read path |
| XL8 Calibrate on the model's own chat / reasoning traces rather than web text, for any quantiser we ever use | optimize.md observation 2 `[paper]` | missing | method; no switch |
| XL9 Our own quantisation run | README: minutes to hours on one consumer GPU for 70B; nothing for 125B + 51B PLE `[unverified]`; needs bf16 weights (hundreds of GB) and a working directory as large as the output | cannot (box, disk) | NOT recommended: use the public 2.18 and 1.66 branches |
| XL10 YAQA Hessians and two-sided LDLQ (-hess, -hreg); QTIP's README points to YAQA | convert.md, QTIP README `[paper]` | missing (Part D lists YAQA as needing backward) | method only; skip |

Priority (rule 8: each gets a default-off switch and a test; none ported or tested now): 1. XL1 + XL2 together (the only lead with a quality number for OUR model; first an engine-free KLD / perplexity of the public 2.18 and 1.66 experts against our IQ2_XS on the same text, then the kernel); 2. XL7 (cheap disk and RAM win, independent of the experts); 3. XL4 (method, offline); 4. XL6 (inside XL1); 5. XL3; 6. XL5 and XL8 (methods, only through XL1); 7. XL10; 8. XL9 (dropped).
Not verified (`[unverified]`): the paper body (2-bit perplexity tables), exllamav3 star count and release history, any author tok/s of EXL3 on a 3060, 3090, 4090 or gfx1100, the 7900 XTX fork's kernels, whether the card's bpw and a GGUF bpw share one base, hours and VRAM to quantise a 125B MoE, and whether the vendored dsv41 kernels handle K = 1.5 and per-expert K on HIP.

## Part S: radiance (Codeberg StillDeadcode/radiance, Apache-2.0, main 04247d112 of 2026-11-20, tag v2.2.0, 119 stars; the RDNA4 study of owner order 2026-11-21; the tree and its issue / PR text were fetched into a new directory outside our repo (C:\Users\iancu\repos\research-radiance), treated as untrusted data, only read: nothing built, run or imported; the box was not touched)

Study only. Not ported, not tested, no engine work. Lead ids are `S1`.. (no hyphen: the ledger's `T-nnn` ids are a different series). Tags: `[code]` read in the radiance source, `[measured]` the AUTHOR's number on THEIR hardware (never ours), `[issue]` text of a Codeberg issue or PR (a claim by its poster, not checked), `[paper]` from a paper, `[derived]` my reading, `[unverified]` not checked. The owner's "radiance for RDNA4" is this repo: its README names `libr4d` (gfx1200 / gfx1201, developed on the Radeon AI PRO R9700), `libr3d` (gfx11xx) and `libhalo` (gfx1151).

Candidates (all found 2026-11-21 by GitHub and Codeberg search; none has a clearer claim to the name):
