| https://github.com/Dyluhn/radiance-exl3, drwolfen/radiance-vllm-r9700 and radiance-vllm-qwen4exp, brainchillz/r9700-tp4-allreduce, ewtodd/radiance-nix and vllm-radiance-nix, Code000-211/radiance-rdna4-isa-suite, bruns20/radiance-wsl, Terrydaktal/vllm-coherence, alperen-albayrak/radiance-vllm-mxfp4, mechovation/Strata-MXFP4 | add-ons, packagings and vLLM variants of radiance on gfx1201; Strata-MXFP4 (MIT) is a Strata-family engine for gfx1201 with one commit and a two-line README | Apache-2.0 / MIT / none / "other" | 2026-09-26 to 2026-11-21 | 0 to 5 | only brainchillz's README read (its GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES data, S1); the rest not read |

Credit and licence: Apache-2.0 allows reuse with the licence text and the NOTICE kept; the code is "Copyright 2026 Deadcode and the radiance contributors" and NOTICE names per-feature authors (tcclaviger, ggz14, slydlake, goodman-b, keptn42, Code000 and others). Nothing of theirs is copied here; every row is written from their text. If a lead below is adopted from their code, README.md section 9 needs a credit line naming the author and commit, the copied file keeps its notice, and vendor/ files carry their own licences (check NOTICE per file first). The hcharbonnier fork is Apache-2.0 too; its journal is a third party's account.

What radiance is, for our purpose `[code]`: it serves Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (our model family) with `placement: expert_tiered`, a pinned host pool for the experts that do not fit and an elastic VRAM split between the expert slab and the KV pool. Its quantisation is its own (IQR-V4: Hadamard-rotated weights, GPTQ, a computed trellis, int8 activations; Flash-Next experts `w4nl64a8h`, container 113 GiB), not a GGUF type, so none of its kernels or containers run on our IQ2_XS pack. RDNA4 is where it is fast; the RDNA3 kernels (`libr3d`) are a later port.

RDNA3 evidence about radiance itself (what matters for the verdict `[issue]` unless tagged): (a) the W7900 (gfx1100, 48 GB) runs Qwen3.8-27B IQ4R-L + DFlash2 at 56-63 tok/s prose and 117-123 code, prefill 1,122-1,140 tok/s at 4.8K tokens (Codeberg issue 440, libr3d patched by the poster on v1.17.0); (b) one RX 7900 XT, MiniCPM5-2B: prefill starts at 200 tok/s and collapses, decode 11-15 tok/s, both Qwen3.8 models fail to load for lack of kernels (issue 427, v1.17.0); (c) Flash-Next IQR-V4 on a W7900, TP1, no drafter: 5.9 tok/s with the reference kernels, 24.2 by default, 31.2 with `RAD_LOGITS_WAVE=1`, "a step issues about a thousand small kernels" (PR 563, open, WIP, 2026-11-20; v2.2.0 itself stops at declaration on gfx1100, issues 462 and 573); (d) the hcharbonnier fork: Flash-Next IQR-V4 on the W7900 at 11.8 tok/s (19.5 with 3 speculative tokens), 3,252-token prefill 5.24-5.28 s (about 620 tok/s) `[measured]`. Our daily preset on the 7900 XT decodes 106.9-113.9 tok/s (short) and 174.3 (edit) and reads 2,405.7 tok/s at 64K (T-822, MOD_PLAN.md section 1), so radiance's gfx1100 path is 4 to 9 times slower than ours at decode: its RDNA4 kernels are not a short cut for us, its IDEAS are the value.

RDNA4 facts they measured, and what they mean for gfx1100 (libr4d/isa, `[measured]` on the R9700 at 300 W unless tagged; RDNA3 column is the owner's hardware list plus `[derived]`):
| fact | RDNA3 (gfx1100) |
| --- | --- |
| `v_wmma_f32_16x16x16_fp8_fp8` sustained 330 TFLOP/s, `iu8` 334, bf16 170; one fp8 WMMA costs about 12.9 VALU issue slots; ceiling formula TFLOP/s = 434 x wmma_slots / (wmma_slots + other instructions) | RDNA4-ONLY for fp8 (no fp8 WMMA on gfx11); iu8 / f16 / bf16 WMMA exist at wave32 16x16x16; their numbers do not transfer (different clock and power limit; iu8 at twice the bf16 rate is an RDNA4 measurement and RDNA3's ratio is not in their text); the issue-slot method itself (count instructions per loop with a disassembler against one calibration number) APPLIES |
| LDS serves 64 B per clock per CU; a VALU instruction costs 1.05-1.4 SIMD clocks serially with the WMMA; a dispatch has a fixed cost of about 4.5 us; most weight-streaming kernels sit at the memory wall (4x more issue capacity than bandwidth) | the shape of the model APPLIES (our T-809 measured 2.98 us per dependent empty launch, T-017 and the decode profile say the window is CPU-wait bound); the constants are gfx1201's |
| `v_swmmac` 2:4 sparse fp8, `global_load_tr_b128` / `ds_read_b64_tr_b16`, `v_cvt_pk_bf16_f32`, no dense 16x16x32 fp8 WMMA | RDNA4-ONLY |
| VOPD (dual issue): their README says the compiler's VOPD pairing is worth 1.4-3.5% on prefill GEMMs and that LLVM 24's pairing order is not repeatable between two builds of the same source; libr3d's README lists VOPD among the things "absent on gfx11" | CONFLICT: the brief (and the gfx11 ISA, `[unverified]` here) has VOPD on RDNA3; the A/B itself settles it (S4) |
| `-mcumode` needed for one GDN kernel on gfx12 and 10% slower for the fp8 prefill GEMM; `s_setprio` neutral | no gfx1100 meaning without a test (Part T T8 lists `-mcumode` as a flag to try) |
| libr3d states that gfx11 has no int8 dot (`__dp4a`, `s32sd4`): "widen to f16, dot2" | WRONG FOR OUR TREE: `src/kernels/cuda/rdna_gr.cu` uses `__builtin_amdgcn_sudot4` on gfx1100 (the decode GEMV, T-009) |
| `v_dot4_i32_i8` is reduced rate on gfx1100: a w4nl MoE down kernel rewritten with it ran +19-20% slower in prefill than 64 `v_mad_i32_i24` (hcharbonnier journal W42m, W7900) `[measured]` | a gfx1100 FACT that matters if we ever write a compute-bound integer prefill kernel on VALU; our decode kernels are bandwidth-bound and our prefill uses F16 WMMA (T-820), so no row |
| PCIe 4.0 x16 link measured 28.5 GB/s h2d (pinned, 256 MiB x 20) on the W7900 (journal W42m) | OUR link: MOD_PLAN.md says PCIe 3.0 forced by the 5700G, probe 13.4 GB/s; Part T T.6 recorded 16.0 GT/s x16 in sysfs during the screen: reconcile before comparing with 28.5 GB/s |

Already in our engine, tested or measured (no row; radiance's idea and our row):
- Next-layer expert prefetch (apply the next routed layer's router to this layer's router input, copy the two likeliest pool-only experts by the second DMA engine, rest when under 30% of copies are used): R9700 one card 29.0 to 28.0 ms/step at one sequence, 101.3 to 95.3 at four, about half the copies used `[measured]`. Ours: the same predictor is the GPU router lookahead, default on (+10%, T-192); the pool-to-VRAM copy side measured -3..-4% (T-528) and the temporal variant `PREFETCH_PREV` has no effect (T-806); `--pcie-frac 0` (T-197, T-268). The resting rule is the only part not tried: S7.
- Zero-copy read of host experts by the GEMM, read in place over the link: our link is half theirs and our miss path is CPU compute, tested (LEADS.md line "Zero-copy host reads ... cannot beat the 13.4 GB/s link", `--pcie-frac`, T-197).
- Heat engine with hysteresis, swap budgets, learned expert profile (`--expert-profile`, `--expert-pin-top`): ours `--adapt-every 1 --adapt-swaps 32` (T-199), swap screens (T-248, T-428, T-827), learned profile +6.7% (T-194), kmiss / protect variants lost (T-789, T-847, T-901). The residency-gated budget of the fork is S8.
- Prefill stager (copy the next layers' experts into a VRAM ring while earlier layers run): `PF_AHEAD`, `PREFILL_RING=192`, `PF_COPY_BATCH` (T-608, T-846, MOD_PLAN.md section 3); bigger prefill steps amortise the expert copy (their one-card 4096 vs 2048-token step: 2,545 vs 1,413 tok/s at 26K tokens `[measured]`): ours `PREFILL_AUTO_MAX=32768` already uses far larger chunks.
- MoE prefill taller blocks (packed 128-row run map for gate_up, 64-row block for the down; -7.8..-8.0% / -12.4..-12.9% per launch at 8192 tokens, +1.6..+2.3% prefill `[measured]`, PR 516): our F16 prompt-expert kernels already have the 128-row tile, `PF_BK64`, half-tile and quarter-tile variants (T-661, T-801, T-737, T-880).
- KV: int8 cache, host-authoritative KV with a resident ring and QSA selection (`--kv int8 --kv-resident 32768`, `KV_HOST_DMA`, `KV_PREFETCH`), a 4-bit KV parity test (`src/kernels/kv_q4_parity.cpp`); elastic KV/expert-slab loan: spec 207 vram-elastic and kvgrow (T-963, T-964).
- Decode MoE GEMV instead of a WMMA tile for 1-4 row runs (libhalo PR 562: 60.4 to 48.9 us per down, +1.2% decode, +4..+7% with MTP3 on gfx1151 `[measured]`): our expert decode is the sudot4 GEMV family (`GR_RDNA`, `RDNA_MMVQ_WAVE`, `EXPDEC`, 9..16-column kernels D6); matrix-core GEMV lost (T-084, T-963 spec 192).
- Speculative decoding: DFlash2 on Flash-Next (the radiance owner closed it as slower than MTP; ours -9% after long prompts, LEADS.md line "DFlash2 as default"), adaptive depth by confidence (ours `--spec-min-p 0.5`, T-118, T-149), load-aware depth for 8 streams (+1.3% / +2.8%, PR 514: single-user box, no row), n-gram / prompt lookup (`--lookup-max-t 12`, T-126).
- Learned-profile pinning, PLE table in host RAM with read-ahead (PLE_WARM, T-750, T-751 not measured), conversation cache with recurrent-state snapshots (T-183, T-946): same ideas; the radiance numbers are TP2 on an R9700.
- ReBAR (their README: without Resizable BAR every dispatch reads its kernel arguments over the link): ours ReBAR 32 GB BAR0 is on (MOD_PLAN.md hardware table).
- Hyper-connection (`hc_read`) kernels: radiance's costs 16% of prefill and about 30% of decode on gfx1100 (journal W40b/W40c, W7900); ours (`HC_UPMIX`, `PF_HCDOWN`, `PF_HC_REGION`) is not in our top kernels (decode profile top: combine_cpu 25.8%, wait_flag 14.8%, rdna_kq 9.0%, expdec_gu 6.8%): a radiance weakness, not a lead.
- Weight quality: lossless bf16 12-bit packing (PR 577), entropy coding of experts: our `EXPERT_ENTROPY_TIER` closed with no decode effect (T-901, T-282).

Leads (rule 8: each gets a default-off switch and a test; none coded or run). Gains are the author's numbers on RDNA4 / gfx1151 / W7900 unless stated; our expected gain is `[derived]` and uncertain, and anything under 2% end to end is below our bar:
| lead | verdict | evidence and our uncertainty | expected gain | effort / risk | default-off switch | test | where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S1 Hardware-queue count 1 and 2, and the pending-event-wait tax: on gfx1201 two other streams holding pending `hipStreamWaitEvent` raised the median tiny-kernel time from 2.5 to 19.4 us, gone with `GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES` of 2 or less, absent on gfx1030 (ROCm legacy-rocm-build issue 6685); brainchillz measured 4-rank decode 210 / 164 / 191 tok/s with `GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES=1` against 139 / 108 / 125 in the slow dispatch state, which 6 of 8 boots entered `[measured, README]` | APPLIES WITH CHANGES (an environment value, no code): gfx1100 is a different ASIC and scheduler path; the effect is `[unverified]` there. Our preset sets 6 (`configs/pancake/flashnext-iq2_xs-fast-hip.json`), only 4 / 6 / 8 were tested (T-010 flat, spec 14 hwq4 / hwq8, T-528 prompts -10..-20% at 8); 1 and 2 never; our engine uses several streams with event waits (copy lanes, IC_WARM side stream, PF_AHEAD, conversation copy streams) | unknown: 0 to a few % decode if the tax exists on gfx1100; the loss side is prefill overlap (fewer queues serialise copy and compute) | XS: env arms, no code; risk: prefill and copy overlap lose | none new: environment `GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES` = 1 and 2 as arms of a spec (rule 8's switch is the env itself); class it in `src/core/conversation_disk_env.def` only if a wrapper key is added | box: first a small microbenchmark (median tiny-kernel us with 0 / 1 / 2 pending-wait streams, graph and stream mode; the reproducer is in ROCm issue 6685), then a spec 14 style screen (short, edit, long, long64k) with `hwq1` / `hwq2` | `bench/specs/14-hip-env.json`, `tools/pancake/probes/` (new probe), the preset env |
| S2 Queue-to-CP-pipe placement and KFD eviction as a source of bimodal runs: radiance issue 478 found that decode is 52% slower exactly when the compute lane and the second lane share one command-processor pipe (119 of 119 requests followed the rule), 74.4-74.9 to 48.9 ms/step with a FIFO instead of LIFO spare list, read from `/sys/kernel/debug/kfd/hqds` and `/sys/class/kfd/kfd/proc/<pid>/stats_<gpuid>/evicted_ms` (KFD evicted the queues once in 11 of 12 starts, after which the slow layout stayed); ROCm issue 6347: decode fixed at one of two speeds (33 or 26 tok/s) at process start `[issue]` | APPLIES WITH CHANGES (diagnostic first): MES-era amdgpu on gfx11 also assigns queues to pipes; whether gfx1100 does it the same way is `[unverified]`. Why it matters here: 10 of 19 screens drifted by more than 5% and a first start after idle differs from later starts (T-817, T-1004, MOD_PLAN.md section 2 screen reading), the root cause was never found | a diagnosis, not a speed: if the layout correlates with the slow starts, it removes the drift that voids our screens; a speed gain only if a slow layout exists (their 21% to 52% are RDNA4 numbers) | S: read-only logging in the sweep harness at every engine start (root for debugfs: owner-run, agents do not touch sudoers); risk none | `STRATA_QUEUE_LAYOUT_LOG=1` (log only: a copy of hqds and evicted_ms into the run's log, default off) | box, no GPU work of its own: at each start of an ordinary sweep log hqds, evicted_ms and tok/s; after about 12 starts check whether pipe sharing or an eviction separates the slow starts | `tools/pancake/box_chains/` (the sweep harness) |
| S3 Chained launches: no AQL barrier bit between dependent kernels; each chained kernel waits in-kernel on its queue's completion counter (radiance `r4d_chain.h`, RAD_CHAIN records). 1.3 ms of a 14 ms DFlash2 step on the 31B at 4 x R9700 `[measured]`; chain-wait tuning -0.32% a step; skipping the scalar-cache invalidate where safe -0.8% (PR 499); without the invalidate 1 launch in 1,800 read a stale scalar value (the hazard to remember) `[code]` | APPLIES WITH CHANGES: the HIP runtime has no barrier-bit control, so it needs a raw ROCr / AQL queue: the same family as LEADS.md Part P L13 (retained PM4 replay) and L18; the new part is the in-kernel counter wait. Our window is 2,248-2,648 dependent kernels at about 2.7-3.0 us per node (T-809, LEADS.md L18) but 40.6% of the decode kernel time is the GPU waiting for CPU rows, so gaps are partly hidden; HIP graphs gave no gain (MOD_PLAN.md section 9, U6) and graph fork / join cost 23-30% (T-528) | at most the L13 ceiling, probably under 3% (their 9% is a GPU-bound dense step, ours is CPU-wait bound) | very large for the engine; M for a probe arm; risk high (stale scalar-cache reads, ROCr queue ownership) | `STRATA_AQL_CHAIN=1` (not before the ceiling clears 3%) | box: extend `tools/pancake/probes/retained_replay.hip.cpp` (T-881, never run) with a no-barrier plus in-kernel-wait arm; ceiling = nodes x (graph - chained) against the 24.8 ms window; stop rule 3% | `tools/pancake/probes/retained_replay.hip.cpp`, `src/core/verify.cpp` (window capture) |
| S4 VOPD pairing on or off, and repeatable code generation: build the HIP sources with `-mllvm -amdgpu-enable-vopd=false` against the default; their README measures the pairing as 1.4-3.5% faster prefill GEMMs and found that two builds of one source differed because the pairing order followed heap addresses (they patch LLVM 24) `[measured, code]` | APPLIES AS IS (compiler option, hardware-independent code): overlaps Part T T8 (flag option) and T10 (ISA audit of VOPD use); the new facts are the measured size and the non-repeatable pairing, which would also explain a "same source, different speed" build-to-build drift. Our toolchain is ROCm 10.0 (clang 23), theirs LLVM 24 (ROCm 10.1, 10.2): whether clang 23 has the repeatability fault is `[unverified]` | 0 to +3% prefill (their range), 0 decode (bandwidth-bound) | S; risk none (build option, exact arithmetic unless a kernel is order-sensitive) | CMake option `STRATA_HIP_NO_VOPD` (OFF = today's flags, byte-identical) | box compile only, no GPU: build one translation unit twice and `cmp` the objects (repeatability), then a prompt A/B (long, long32k, long64k) with the option ON; the ISA audit of T10 first shows whether VOPD pairs exist in `rdna_kq` and the prompt kernels | `CMakeLists.txt` (HIP flags) |
| S5 Chunked GDN prefill (WY form, 64-chunk) on the gfx11 wave matrix: radiance ports libhalo's `kkt_solve` and `chunk_scan` kernels to gfx11 WMMA in libr3d (`radiance:libr3d/r3dh_gdn_kkt_solve.hip`, `r3dh_gdn_chunk_scan.hip`, `r3dh_gdn_wmma.h`) and checked them against the reference on gfx1151: kkt solve to 8e-5, scan to 4e-3, update to 6e-5 `[code, measured]` | APPLIES WITH CHANGES: this is LEADS.md A31 / B42 whose premise T-746 found unrunnable on gfx11 ("HIP has the FP32 scan only"); a gfx11 WMMA fragment layout is now in print (lanes 0-15 replicate into 16-31, `k = 8*(e>>2) + 4*(lane>>4) + (e&3)`). Evidence against: chunked WY was 0.73x on the 3060 (T-528), GDN was 11.9% of prefill there (T-087), no 7900 XT GDN share is recorded `[unverified]` | prefill: our GDN share x kernel speedup; if GDN is about 12% of prefill and the new kernel is 1.5x, +4%; could be zero or negative | L (port two kernels, parity vs our FP32 scan, tolerance study); risk medium (not bit-exact, lossy class) | `STRATA_GDN_CHUNK_WMMA=1` (default off) | host: parity against the FP32 scan with `tools/pancake/probes/gdn_chunk_model.py`; box: rocprof of the GDN share on long prompts first (rule: kernel gain x share >= 2% before end to end), then A/B long32k / long64k with the quality gate | `src/prefill/` (the sm_70 `gdn_chunk.cu` path), `tools/pancake/probes/gdn_chunk64_rule.py` |
| S6 GDN recurrent state stored as f16 (`RADIANCE_GDN_STATE_F16`): halves the state pool, its host checkpoints and the state traffic; lossy: KL +0.9..+1.1% with fp8 KV on Qwen3.8-27B (MXFP4, TP2); decode 1 stream +0.6%, 4 streams +2.6%, 16 streams +10.1% without speculation, -3.2% / -2.5% with 7 drafts at 1 / 4 streams `[measured]`, commit 4d68a01a0 | APPLIES WITH CHANGES: Flash-Next has 36 GDN layers; the benefit that is ours is the conversation-cache snapshot (a recurrent-state copy per parked turn, restored on a warm turn, T-183, T-946) rather than decode (one stream, speculative: their f16 decode result at 7 drafts is negative). Snapshot-only storage (f32 live, f16 stored) is a variant they did not measure `[derived]` | warm-turn restore bytes halved (size of the snapshot is not in the ledger `[unverified]`); decode about 0 | M; risk: quality (a lossy class change: quality gate + token forcing) | `STRATA_GDN_STATE_F16=1` (live state) and `STRATA_SNAP_STATE_F16=1` (snapshot only), both default off | host: round-trip error of the state; box: spec on a replayed agent session (replay_turns.py) for time to first token, then the quality gate | `src/core/conversation_state.cpp`, `src/core/conversation_snapshot.cpp`, GDN state allocation in `src/core/verify.cpp` |
| S7 Resting rule for a predictive copy: radiance pauses its next-layer copies when under 30% of the last 2,048 were used, for 8,192 passes, and resumes | APPLIES WITH CHANGES: our measured negatives (T-528 -3..-4%, T-806 no effect, pre-read costs 3%) ran with the copy always on; a usage-gated copy was never tried; our link is slower than theirs | 0 to +1% `[derived]`; their gain needed about 50% of copies used (-3.4% / -5.9% step time) | S (counter plus gate around the existing copy); risk low | `STRATA_PREFETCH_REST=1` (default off, with `STRATA_PREFETCH_PREV` or the pool pre-read as the copy it gates) | box: spec 133 style arms with the rest rule; log the used share first (if it is under 30% the lead is closed) | `src/core/verify.cpp`, `include/strata/core/expert_cache.hpp` |
| S8 Residency-gated swap budget: 64 swaps per step until the experts of 60% of the moveable bytes are resident and a broad histogram pass (4,096 selections) has run, then 16 for 512 steps; -21% link bytes per token (537 to 423 MiB), decode -1.75%; a global 16 was +6.3% worse at 92 tokens (hcharbonnier journal W40c, W7900, gfx1100, bit-identical text) `[measured]` | APPLIES WITH CHANGES: ours is a fixed 32 (T-199) with fixed-budget screens (T-248, T-428, T-827) and `ADAPT_SWAPS_AUTO` (T-221); a schedule keyed to residency was not tried. Their model is decode-latency-bound at 3,252 tokens, ours CPU-miss-bound | at most +1.75% (their own); likely inside noise | S; risk low | `STRATA_ADAPT_SWAPS_SCHED=1` (default off) | box: screen short and edit with the schedule against `--adapt-swaps 32`; log promoted MiB per token | `src/core/expert_cache.cpp`, `src/program/generate.cpp` (adapt_swaps) |
| S9 No separate tail chunk: a prompt cut as 896 + 76 tokens makes the 76-row chunk read 77% of the experts (0.65-0.75 s of 2.04 s); `--tail-anchor off` gave 1,409 against 2,003 ms (1.42x) at 972 tokens on the W7900; `prefill-span-first` -2.8..-19.4% time to first token and `prefill-tail-snapshot off` -1.2..-6.3% on single prompts, at the price of slow follow-up turns (166 to 441 ms) (PR 518, journal W34 / W42m) `[measured]` | APPLIES WITH CHANGES: our small deltas (up to about 400 tokens) already go to the CPU pool (`PREFILL_CPU_SHARE`), the 0.6-0.8 s floor of a warm turn (MOD_PLAN.md section 1) may or may not be a tail chunk, and `PREFILL_EQUAL_TAIL` (D13, +-1.7%) already evens the last chunk; whether our planner cuts a snapshot chunk at all is `[unverified]` | prompt time on cold single prompts of 1-4K tokens: up to their 1.2-1.4x; warm agent turns about 0 | M (planner and snapshot invariants); risk medium (resume equality) | `STRATA_PF_TAIL_MERGE=1` (default off) | host: chunk-plan unit test (`include/strata/program/prefill_plan.hpp`); box: TTFT at long1k / long3k and the warm-turn replay | `include/strata/program/prefill_plan.hpp`, `src/program/generate.cpp` |
| S10 QSA indexer launches skipped below the exactness bound (2,051 tokens): below it every complete block is selected whatever the scores, yet the query norm + rope, `qsa_score` and `qsa_select` still ran at every full-attention layer (36 dependent launches a decode / verify step on Flash-Next); skipped, +1.1..+1.5% below 2K tokens, +0.0% at 7.7K, bit-identical (PR 496) `[measured]` | APPLIES WITH CHANGES: we already return early inside the score kernel when the width is 2,051 (`STRATA_QSA_EARLY_EXIT`, `src/kernels/cuda/native_qsa_score.cu`) but still launch; our QSA scores + top-k are 1.4% of the 24.8 ms window (T-017); 36 launches x 2.8 us is about 0.1 ms | +0.4% at most on short contexts `[derived]` | S; risk low (exact) | `STRATA_QSA_SKIP_BELOW_BOUND=1` (default off) | host: compare the selection table bytes; box: screen short only (the long cases are past the bound) | `src/kernels/cuda/qsa_select.cu`, `src/core/verify.cpp` |
| S11 Launch order of the small decode ops (PR 498, Flash-Next): router weight read beside the mixer reduction, k and v projections ahead of q-and-gate, the indexer keys behind the attention launches, the shared down projection before the routed down, chain polling by grid size; -2.5% ms/step, +3.3% tok/s over 156 paired requests, texts identical; the stated cause: a verify pass is about 900 launches a rank in about 10 ms against 6.4 ms of weight reads `[measured]` | APPLIES WITH CHANGES (a principle, not a kernel): our `IC_WARM_MB`, `IC_WARM_DEFER` (T-018, T-643, T-895, T-963) are the side-stream weight read; the launch-order part (which small kernel goes first, so the next op's weights are in flight) is not in the ledger; the launch-fusion switches screened as noise (T-604, T-612, T-614, T-616) and our window is CPU-wait bound | under 1% `[derived]` | S to M; risk low (exact) | `STRATA_DECODE_ORDER=1` (default off) | box: screen short / edit; a ledger-grade measurement needs a kernel-time view too (`tools/pancake/kernel_axis.py`, T-881) | `src/core/verify.cpp`, `src/core/mtp.cpp` |
| S12 Sequence-level verification of the coupled MTP chain: block verification (arXiv 2403.10444) and traversal verification (arXiv 2505.12398), lossless, accept at least as many drafts as token-wise; traversal +2.1% tok/s overall (95% CI +0.3..+3.7), +4.6% (+1.9..+7.4) at T 1.0 with top-k 0, nothing measurable at T 0.7 or with a top-k / top-p filter, block verification neutral on speed (PR 500, 117 paired requests) `[measured, paper]` | APPLIES AS IS (algorithm, no hardware dependence), but our sampling is temperature 0.1, top_p 0.95, top_k 20 where their effect vanishes | about 0 at our default; +2..+5% only on a high-temperature, unfiltered preset | M (`src/core/mtp.cpp` verify plus the device accept) ; risk medium (exactness of the output law needs a chi-square test like theirs) | `STRATA_DRAFT_VERIFY=block\|traversal` (default token) | host: distribution test; box: a T 1.0 top_k 0 arm only | `src/core/mtp.cpp`, `src/core/verify.cpp` (device accept: `STRATA_DEVICE_ACCEPT`) |
| S13 MTP-head experts kept in host memory (`--draft-expert-placement host`), so their VRAM goes to the main expert slab | APPLIES WITH CHANGES: our drafter loads its 512 experts to VRAM (`src/core/mtp.cpp`, 675 MiB with `--mtp-q4` variants; `--mtp-q4 all` screened under an ORDER EFFECT, T-681); on the W7900 650 MiB more slab was -3.3% pool and -1.0% prefill, decode not reported (journal W42m) | about +1% at best; the draft pass over the link would be slower | S to M; risk: draft latency | `STRATA_MTP_EXPERTS_HOST=1` (default off) | box: re-run the T-681 `--mtp-q4` arm with the base-late drift check, then this | `src/core/mtp.cpp` |
| S14 Vocabulary-probability-weighted lm_head: head rows grouped into four bit-width classes by the row's token-probability-weighted output error (4.0 / 4.5 / 5.0 b mean), decoded in one launch; the head is about a tenth of every IQR-V4 entry's bytes `[code, measured]` (IQR-V4) | IDEA ONLY (a quantiser method, lossy): needs a requantisation of our head; our `HEAD_MIX_MULTI` and head quant types are GGUF's | head bytes -10..-20% at equal KL `[unverified]`; decode effect small (the head is read once per verify window) | L (tool in `tools/iq_pack.py`, kernel, quality gate); risk quality | `STRATA_HEAD_VOCAB_CLASSES=<file>` (default off) | host: KL of the head alone on `bench/quality` text; box only after a pack exists | `tools/iq_pack.py`, `src/core/native_head.cpp` |
| S15 Protected hot experts at a higher precision (their Flash-Next recipe keeps ten experts bf16; more protected experts cost +4% bytes for 7% less error; five-bit down projections recover about 80% of what bf16 down projections would, +4% decode and -19% prefill cost on two cards) | IDEA ONLY (needs our own pack; ours is ISTA-DASLab's GSQ-RCO IQ2_XS, LEADS.md Part R): combine with our learned profile (`data/expert-profile-pancake.bin`) so the experts held in VRAM are the ones given extra bits | quality (not speed): unknown for IQ2_XS | L; risk: new pack, bytes | none until a pack exists (an expert type in the cache) | host: KL by layer band on our pack, as MOE-W4's band table does | `tools/iq_pack.py`, `include/strata/core/expert_cache.hpp` |
| S16 IQR-V4 / computed trellis family as an expert format (Hadamard-rotated weights, GPTQ inside a tail-biting trellis of 2^16 states, value = byte-sum of a hash, one f32 scale per row, int8 activations): Qwen3.8-27B IQ2R-V4 2.47 bpw KL 0.0611, IQ3R-V4 3.47 bpw 0.0150; Flash-Next experts `w4nl64a8h` mean KL 0.0789 over all positions, 0.0239 over text and assistant turns, against a bf16 floor of 0.0399 / 0.0111 `[measured]` | IDEA ONLY and the same family as Part R (EXL3, XL1, XL5): no radiance container fits our 20 GB card and RAM (113 GiB, no IQ2 class for Flash-Next); their gfx1100 speed is 11.8-31 tok/s (W7900 figures above), so their kernels are no template for our decode; the part Part R lacked is a public KL table by band and a quantiser with act-order GPTQ inside the trellis | quality per byte at about 2.5 bpw: their 27B IQ2R-V4 beats UD-IQ2_S by 0.061 vs 0.130 KL at equal bits `[measured]`; for our pack unknown | XL (new pack, kernels, calibration data: not recommended now) | none | host: KL of our IQ2_XS pack against their 2.47 bpw trellis only if a public Flash-Next IQR container at about 2.5 bpw appears | n/a |

Not leads (the evidence is complete): (1) RDNA4-ONLY: fp8 / bf8 WMMA, `v_swmmac` sparse, transposed global and LDS loads, hardware bf16 pack, the fp8 prefill tile and the all-reduce kernels' behaviour at PCIe 5. (2) NEEDS HARDWARE WE LACK: two-rank and four-rank P2P all-reduce (`ar_oneshot_*`, wht6 wire), tensor parallel at 2 / 3 / 4 / 8 ranks, TP2's second-lane weight reads beside the all-reduces, the second DMA engine of an R9700 under TP1: parked with our other two-GPU items (MOD_PLAN.md section 8). (3) DROPPED BY EVIDENCE: DFlash2 for Flash-Next (their owner: slower than MTP), speculative-depth tuning for many streams, prefill scheduling for several clients (`--prefill-interleave`), model hosting features (fleet YAML, dashboard).

Top 3 recommended leads (cheapest first, each answers a question that blocks other measurements):
1. S1 plus S2 together: `GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES` 1 and 2 as arms, and the queue-layout log at every engine start. Zero code for S1, read-only for S2; the pair tests whether the dispatch-state effect that cost RDNA4 users 20-50% exists on gfx1100, and S2 may explain the screen drift that voids most of our A/B runs.
2. S4: the VOPD build option. One compile flag plus a repeatability check; it also feeds Part T's ISA audit (T10) and flag option (T8), and a measured 1.4-3.5% on prefill GEMMs is the largest exact prefill number in the radiance tree that needs no kernel work.
3. S6 in its snapshot-only form (`STRATA_SNAP_STATE_F16`) if the recurrent-state snapshot is a large part of a warm turn (check the snapshot size first); otherwise S5, the gfx11 chunked-GDN prefill, as the one real kernel project the radiance tree offers us (its WMMA layout and tolerances are the missing piece of A31).

What needs the box (the box is busy with the flag screen and was not used: no ssh, no test, no build; this PC has a GTX 1660 Ti and no gfx1100): S1 (microbenchmark and spec arms), S2 (hqds and evicted_ms need root on the box), S3 (probe arm), S4 (compile on the box, then A/B), S5 (rocprof share and kernel parity), S7, S8, S9, S10, S11, S13 (A/B screens), S6 (replay and the quality gate). Host-only on this PC: S6 round-trip error of the state, S9 chunk-plan unit test, S12 distribution test.

Not verified `[unverified]`: whether gfx1100 shows S1's pending-wait tax and S2's pipe sharing (their evidence is gfx1201 on MES; gfx1030, a non-MES part, is clean); the LLVM version and VOPD repeatability of our clang 23; the size of one recurrent-state snapshot of Flash-Next IQ2_XS; our GDN share of prefill on the 7900 XT; Codeberg star and issue counts as of 2026-11-21 (API); every radiance number is the author's or a poster's on their hardware; I did not read radiance's docs/KERNELS.md (1.6 MB of generated tables, no gfx1100 rows found by grep), the per-kernel headers beyond libr4d/isa and libr3d's README, or the paper bodies.

## Part T: ROCm 10.x release study (ROCm Core SDK 10.0.0, 10.1.0, 10.2.0; HIP 7.15 to 7.17; read 2026-11-21; docs fetched as text into a new directory outside our repo (C:\Users\iancu\repos\research-rocm, untrusted data, nothing from it run); nothing built, no box setting changed; the Part letter S is held for the radiance / RDNA4 study)

Study only, owner order 2026-11-21 ("study the ROCm 10.2 release changes and commands and HIP, find what we can apply, also ROCm/HIP features not used yet for RDNA3"). Not ported, not tested. Lead ids here are `T1`.. (no hyphen: the ledger's `T-nnn` ids are a different series). Tags: `[doc]` read in the official release notes or changelog, `[box]` read-only look at the box on 2026-11-21 12:00 (nothing started that computes on the GPU, nothing written), `[code]` our tree grepped at 4e77019a6, `[derived]` my reading, `[unverified]` not checked or not stated by AMD.

### T.0 Which release is "ROCm 10.2": DECISION
- ROCm 10.2.0 EXISTS. It is the newest release: "ROCm Core SDK 10.2.0", dated 2026-11-18, Linux and Windows, built with TheRock (the build system since 7.14); HIP 7.17.0, LLVM 24.0.0, hipBLASLt 1.5.0, rocBLAS 5.8.0, AMD SMI 27.2.0, ROCprofiler-SDK 1.4.2, ROCm Compute Profiler 3.10.0, ROCm Systems Profiler 1.10.0, rocWMMA 2.2.2, CK 1.3.0, MIOpen 3.6.2, rocPRIM 4.8.0, RCCL 2.32.3 `[doc]` (https://rocm.docs.amd.com/en/docs-10.2.0/about/release-notes.html; the header of the HIP environment-variable page reads "ROCm Core SDK 10.2.0, HIP 7.17.0"; Phoronix news "AMD ROCm 10.2 Released ..."). The owner's "10.2" is therefore NOT a typo and not 6.2 / 7.2. The numbering went 7.2.x (Jan to May 2026), 7.14.0 (2026-07-16, TheRock, the last "7.x"), then 10.0.0, 10.1.0, 10.2.0. The newest tag on the ROCm/ROCm GitHub releases list at the time of fetching was still rocm-7.14.0, so GitHub lags the docs site: the docs site is the authority. A 10.3.0 nightly is named in the 10.2.0 known issues (WSL2 workaround), no release yet.
- Releases newer than our box, all read: 10.1.0 (HIP 7.16.0, ROCprofiler-SDK 1.4.1, rocBLAS 5.7.0, MIOpen 3.6.1, AMD SMI 27.1.0) and 10.2.0. The 10.0.0 notes were read for what our box already has.
- Our box: ROCm 10.0.0 `[box]`: packages `amdrocm-*10.0` 10.0.0-4 (core metapackage `amdrocm-core10.0-gfx1100`, `amdrocm-blas10.0-gfx1100`, `amdrocm-ck10.0`, `amdrocm-dnn10.0-gfx1100`), installed under /opt/rocm/core-10.0 (a versioned tree, so a second tree core-10.2 can sit beside it); `hipcc --version`: HIP version 7.15.26333, AMD clang 23.0.0git (ROCm/llvm-project 8f497e0992); libamdhip64.so.7.15.26333, libhipblaslt.so.1.4 (hipBLASLt 1.4.x, our table key 100401), librocblas.so.5.6, libhsa-runtime64.so.1.21.0; amd-smi 27.0.0 ("ROCm version: 10.0.0"); rocprofv3 1.3.5; kernel 7.0.0-43-generic; the amdgpu driver packages are the 7.2.70200 line. The engine runs with LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/opt/rocm/core-10.0/lib (`tools/pancake/abtest.py` line 1033). The compiler is LLVM 23 on the box against 24 in 10.2.
- Delta box (10.0) to newest (10.2) that touches us: HIP 7.15 to 7.17, hipBLASLt 1.4 to 1.5.0, rocBLAS 5.6 to 5.8.0, LLVM 23 to 24, rocprofv3 1.3.5 to SDK 1.4.2, amd-smi 27.0 to 27.2. Everything else in the notes (MI300 / MI350 / gfx1250 / gfx950 / RDNA4 kernels, RCCL, rocSPARSE, rocFFT, rocALUTION, hipFile, virtualization, WSL2, Ryzen APUs, Windows) does not touch a gfx1100 Linux box (rows T24, T26).
- Only two gfx1100 lines in the 10.1 and 10.2 notes: hipBLASLt 1.5.0 "improved fp16 and bf16 NN GEMM performance on gfx1100 with Origami libraries" and MIOpen "fixed invalid CK perf-config entries in the gfx1201 and gfx1100 SystemDBs" `[doc]`. The 10.0.0 notes: Level 3 gemm on gfx11 bypasses the per-precision gemv heuristics, refreshed gfx1100 MIOpen SystemDBs. The notes give no compiler change list (the component table says LLVM 24.0.0; one future symlink move), so every compiler flag below is `[unverified]` against LLVM 24.

### T.1 Already ours (no row, do not re-propose; ids from TEST_LEDGER.md)
hipBLASLt solution table for the prompt GEMMs `STRATA_HIPBLASLT_TUNING` (T-096: 1,345 to 1,850-1,920 tok/s, ADOPTED; file tools/hip/gfx1100-hipblaslt-100401.txt; the loader keys the file by `hipblasLtGetVersion()` and the card: src/prefill/gemm.cu `create_hipblaslt_state`, src/prefill/hipblaslt_tuning.hpp) and the descriptor cache `STRATA_HIPBLASLT_DESC_CACHE`; `STRATA_GR_RDNA` family, `STRATA_RDNA_MMVQ_WAVE=3` (wave32 arm dropped, T-072), `STRATA_RDNA_Q6K` (T-009, T-078, T-079, T-699), `STRATA_VERIFY_ECAP` (T-269 to T-271, T-588, T-589, kept T-990), `STRATA_AMD_COMMUNITY_TUNING` (T-985), the PF_* prompt kernels and `PF_F16` (T-820), WMMA attention `STRATA_ATTN_WMMA` / `STRATA_HIP_WMMA` (T-019, T-023, T-031, T-032; build macro STRATA_WMMA_GFX11 in cmake/hip_backend.cmake), HIP graphs (the window graph with hipGraphInstantiateWithFlags and hipGraphExecUpdate, `STRATA_MTP_ONE_GRAPH`; graph fork/join measured -23 to -30%, T-528), VMM (`STRATA_HIP_VRAM_ELASTIC` T-896; `STRATA_VMM_ZERO_BACK` T-857, `vmm_test` failed on gfx1100 in T-950), the pinned arena with cudaHostAlloc / cudaHostRegister (about 150 call sites), `GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES` (T-010 flat; 8 queues -10 to -20% on prompts, T-528), HIP_FORCE_DEV_KERNARG (the runtime default is already 1 `[doc]`), SDMA use (MOD_PLAN.md host-tuning note: `HSA_ENABLE_SDMA=0` conflicts with the measured SDMA gains; `STRATA_PF_COPY_BATCH` coded, spec 149 / T-919 inconclusive, item 48), stream priorities (`STRATA_MTP_HIP_PRIO` T-141 / T-142 no difference, dropped; `STRATA_GRAPH_PRIO`, `STRATA_COPY_HIP_PRIO` coded only), fast-math as a build option `STRATA_HIP_FAST_MATH` (T-813, T-815: -2.4 / -3.0% against base-late, no gain) and the scheduling fence `STRATA_PA_SCHED_FENCE` (T-815, no gain), rocprofv3 kernel trace (T-017, T-020, T-021, T-029, T-033; the kernel shares of T-020: combine_cpu_k10_vec4 38.3%, rdna_kq about 10%, expdec_gu + native_down about 13%, rdna_gr_fused 5.6%, bf16 mmvf 5.1%, wait_flag_copy 4.1%, gaps over 1 ms 525 ms of 3,638 ms wall). Grep of the tree `[code]`: no hipMemAdvise, hipMemPrefetchAsync, hipMallocAsync, memory pools, hipExtMallocWithFlags, -mcumode, -mwavefrontsize64, amdgpu-sched flags or LTO anywhere; `__launch_bounds__` in 76 source files, `amdgpu_waves_per_eu` / `amdgpu_flat_work_group_size` in 4; no bf16x2 comparison intrinsic (the HIP 7.17 `__hgt2` / `__hbneu2` / `__hisnan2` fix cannot change our results).

### T.2 What 10.1 and 10.2 change that matters to a gfx1100 decode + prompt engine `[doc]`
1. hipBLASLt 1.5.0: the Origami retune of fp16 / bf16 NN GEMM on gfx1100; tuning-file handling changed (rows trusted one at a time, `kernel_name` recorded in HIPBLASLT_TUNING_FILE, rows without a name used only when the file's Git Version line matches the running build; index 0 was wrongly rejected before; a file with several entries for one problem used only the first); solution catalogs now stay compressed on disk as `.dat.zlib` and are decompressed on first use ("can add initial overhead", gfx942 example 194 MB instead of 3.8 GB); workspace limits above 4 GiB - 1 are clamped; `HIPBLASLT_CHECK_SYNCHRONIZER` debug variable; `TENSILE_PRECISE_SM_TARGET` removed. Our engine does NOT use HIPBLASLT_TUNING_OVERRIDE_FILE: it picks a solution index itself, so the per-row trust rules do not apply, but the index-scope rule does (T2).
2. HIP 7.16 / 7.17: graph execution update faster ("eliminating quadratic scaling in certain graph update scenarios"); stream priority DISABLED ("priority streams are currently not supported"); hipMemcpy3DBatchAsync cheaper SDMA submission; hipMemcpyBatchAsync no longer blocks the host behind a long kernel; hipMallocAsync / hipEventSynchronize deadlock fixed; capture fixes (hipStreamEndCapture segfault on multi-stream capture; legacy-stream hipMemset / hipMemcpy no longer invalidates ThreadLocal / Relaxed captures); host-NUMA VMM; allocation properties recoverable from imported VMM handles; `HIP_TARGET_API_VERSION` compile-time API pin; new hipModuleEnumerateFunctions, hipLibraryGetModule, hipKernelSetAttributeForDevice; `HIPRTC_USE_RUNTIME_UNBUNDLER` removed; `__hip_bfloat162` comparison fixes.
3. HIP 7.15 (already on our box): hipEventRecord coalescing, hipMemcpy2D small-row path, hipMemcpyBatchAsync per-device split, `hipMemGetDefaultMemPool`, cooperative_groups scans, BatchMemOp capture, hipMemMap / hipMemUnmap null-stream deadlock fix.
4. Profilers: rocprofv3 kernel replay (beta, 10.1), HIP event tracing `--hip-event-trace` (10.1), HIP graph per-node attribution `--hip-graph-trace` (10.0, on the box), live attach with Advanced Thread Trace and the `rocprof-trace-decoder` Python API (10.0), SPM device counting (10.2: gfx1200 / 1201 / 1250 only), `rocsys` unified command for the Systems Profiler (10.2), ROCm Compute Profiler 3.10 (breaking CLI change: `-d` is now the output directory, use `--kernel-iteration-range`; roofline and memory chart pages name Instinct and gfx115x only).
5. AMD SMI 27.1 / 27.2: `amdsmi_get_energy_count` valid on Radeon (bare metal), AMPP power-profile API and CLI (`amd-smi static --ampp`, `amd-smi set --ampp-activate`), `amd-smi set --power-cap` applied per GPU, `set -L mclk|fclk max` rounding, exit-code / CSV / error-format changes (breaking for parsers). The Python library is on PyPI.

### T.4 Deprecations and breaking changes that could hurt us (from the 10.1 / 10.2 notes)
1. Stream priority disabled (HIP 7.17): row T5, no effect today.
2. hipBLASLt solution indices are build-scoped and the table file name carries the version: a ROCm upgrade without a new table drops the prompt path to hipBLASEx with no message (T2).
3. hipBLASLt catalogs unpack on first use (T4).
4. ROCm paths: the install root is versioned (core-10.x), so every script that names core-10.0 breaks on a move (T1); in 10.2 the LLVM runtime libraries move one level deeper (lib/llvm/lib to lib/llvm/lib/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu, compatibility symlinks kept until the next major version): a build script that links compiler-rt by path needs the new directory later.
5. `HIPRTC_USE_RUNTIME_UNBUNDLER` is gone (we set none); `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT_BATCHED` is gone (we set none; rocBLAS is only the gfx103x table, STRATA_ROCBLAS_TUNING).
6. `__hip_bfloat162` comparison results changed (no use in our tree, section T.1).
7. amd-smi CLI exit codes, a third CSV column and structured errors changed: the parsers listed in T15 need a check on 10.2.
8. rocprof-compute `-d` now means the output directory (scripts that use it as a dispatch filter break): we do not use rocprof-compute (T13).
9. The box runs the amdgpu 7.2.70200 driver packages and kernel 7.0.0, which matches the 10.1 / 10.2 Ubuntu 24.04.5 HWE kernel 7.0 line `[doc, box]`; the exact firmware / driver pairing for 10.2 sits in the notes' kernel-driver table and was not read for gfx1100 `[unverified]`.

### T.5 Top 5 recommended leads (rule 8: none is coded or tested here)
1. T1 + T2 together: install 10.2 beside 10.0 (owner), then a new gfx1100 hipBLASLt table for 100500 from `tools/hip/tune_hipblaslt.cpp` and the loader warning. Reason: the only 10.2 change with a stated gfx1100 gain (Origami fp16 / bf16 NN GEMM) sits on the prompt path, where the table is worth +38% (T-096), and a missing table is silent.
2. T3 (hipBLASLt heuristic arm) with T4 (warm-up): finds whether the per-version table can go, and removes the first-request cost of the compressed catalogs.
3. T12 + T11 (timeline and counters with rocprofv3 on SDK 1.4.2: event trace, graph trace, kernel replay): the first measurement that says why 31% of kernel time is a CPU wait (T-021) and which hot kernels are compute-bound; profiling only, safe.
4. T9 + T10 (resource report and ISA audit): no runtime risk, shows spills, occupancy and VOPD use of rdna_kq, gr_fused and combine_cpu_k10_vec4 before any flag (T8) is tried.
5. T14 (COMPUTE power profile, 257 to 290 W power limit; owner action): the only never-tested host lever for gfx1100 decode; needs root, one A/B.
Then T7 (copy batching on the faster runtime), T8 (flag option, after T9 / T10), T17, T18, T23, T25.

### T.6 Commands to run later on the box (read-only first; the first block is the read-only look already done on 2026-11-21 12:00, nothing else was run)
Read-only, no GPU compute, no sudo (safe during a screen):
- `cat /opt/rocm/core-10.0/.info/version` (the plain /opt/rocm/.info/version does not exist: the tree is /opt/rocm/core-10.0), `/opt/rocm/core-10.0/bin/hipcc --version | head -4`, `/opt/rocm/core-10.0/bin/amd-smi version`, `dpkg -l | grep amdrocm`
- `/opt/rocm/core-10.0/bin/amd-smi static --asic --limit`, `/opt/rocm/core-10.0/bin/amd-smi static --bus`
- `cat /sys/class/drm/card1/device/power_dpm_force_performance_level /sys/class/drm/card1/device/pp_power_profile_mode /sys/class/drm/card1/device/current_link_speed /sys/class/drm/card1/device/current_link_width` (card1 = the RX 7900 XT, device id 0x744c; reading 2026-11-21: level high, active profile BOOTUP_DEFAULT, link 16.0 GT/s x16 during the running screen, while MOD_PLAN.md states PCIe 3.0 for the bus: reconcile with `lspci -vv -s <bus>` LnkSta under load, since H2D expert streaming speed depends on it)
- `LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/opt/rocm/core-10.0/lib /opt/rocm/core-10.0/bin/rocprofv3-avail list --pmc` (counters of gfx1100), `rocprofv3-avail info --pmc`, `rocprofv3 --version`
- `/opt/rocm/core-10.0/bin/amd-smi metric -g 0 --energy --power --clock` (the energy counter on Radeon is a 10.2 feature, expect N/A on 10.0)
After the flag screen (box idle; each its own owner-approved step):
- Owner, root: `sudo apt install amdrocm-core10.2-gfx1100 amdrocm10.2-gfx1100` (names follow the 10.0 packages; check `apt-cache search amdrocm | grep 10.2` first) so that /opt/rocm/core-10.2 appears beside core-10.0 (T1).
- Build the same commit against it: `ROCM=/opt/rocm/core-10.2` and `-DCMAKE_HIP_ARCHITECTURES=gfx1100` in a new build directory (the pattern of tools/pancake/box_chains/bench-updatedAA.sh with ROCM overridden), then `ctest` for the HIP tests.
- Tune the table on 10.2 (T2): tools/hip/tune_hipblaslt.cpp built against core-10.2, written to tools/hip/gfx1100-hipblaslt-100500.txt, then `STRATA_HIPBLASLT_TUNING=<that file>` for the prompt A/B at 14K / 32K / 64K.
- Profiling (T11, T12, T13): `rocprofv3 --kernel-trace --stats --hip-trace --hip-event-trace --hip-graph-trace -d <out> -- <engine command>`; counters: `rocprofv3 --pmc SQ_WAVES GRBM_GUI_ACTIVE --replay-mode kernel --kernel-replay-beta-enabled -d <out> -- <engine command>` (10.1+ only; counter names from `rocprofv3-avail list --pmc`); system timeline: `rocsys -- <engine command>` (10.2); thread trace: `rocprofv3 --att ...` (read `--help` first; gfx1100 support not stated).
- Resource report and ISA (T9, T10): configure a build directory with `-DCMAKE_HIP_FLAGS="-Rpass-analysis=kernel-resource-usage"` and keep the build log; disassemble the gfx1100 code object with the ROCm `llvm-objdump` (offline).
- Owner, root, only with a revert plan (T14): `echo 5 | sudo tee /sys/class/drm/card1/device/pp_power_profile_mode` (COMPUTE; check first whether `power_dpm_force_performance_level` must be `manual` for the write), `sudo amd-smi set --power-cap <W>` within 231-290 W; revert with `echo 0` and the stock cap 257 W.

### T.7 Not verified (`[unverified]`)
LLVM 24 flag names and effects for gfx11 (`-amdgpu-sched-strategy`, `-mcumode`, `-foffload-lto`, `--offload-compress`); whether rocprofv3 ATT and kernel replay work on gfx1100 and on a 20 GB-resident engine; whether rocprof-compute supports gfx1100; the size of the gfx1100 hipBLASLt catalogs and the first-use unpack time; whether the rows of tools/hip/gfx1100-hipblaslt-100500.txt (tuned on the 10.2.0a nightly) carry the GA 10.2.0 library's indices; the hipBLASLt grouped-GEMM path on gfx1100; HSA_XNACK and HSA_ENABLE_SDMA are runtime-level variables that the HIP environment page does not list; gfx1100 behaviour under HSA_XNACK=1; the effect of the compute profile on a 7900 XT; amd-smi ampp support on gfx11; the GA 10.2 driver / firmware pairing for Radeon. The release-note text was read from the docs site's markdown source and changelog (10.0.0, 10.1.0, 10.2.0) plus the HIP environment-variable page of 10.2.0.

## Part U: radiance-rdna3 in depth (github.com/hcharbonnier/radiance-rdna3, Apache-2.0, main f3e4893f of 2026-11-20, 0 stars, 0 forks, 0 issues, 0 PRs, no discussions; a single-card Radeon PRO W7900 gfx1100 port of radiance with a work journal; owner order 2026-11-21: "go much deeper" than Part S; the GitHub tarball (84 MB, 1,930 files) and the API JSON (repo, commits, branches, issues, pulls, releases) were fetched into a new empty directory outside our repo (C:\Users\iancu\repos\research-hcharbonnier), treated as untrusted data, only read: nothing built, run or imported; upstream radiance PR 563 read through the Codeberg API; the box was not touched)

Study only. Not ported, not tested, no engine work. Lead ids are `U1`.. (no hyphen: the ledger's `T-nnn` ids are a different series; Part S ids `S1`..`S16` are cited as such). Tags: `[code]` read in their tree (their files are written `hcr:path` below, the same convention as the other foreign parts), `[journal]` an entry of their work journal (a claim by its author, normally with command, clocks and tree state; entry names W34, W42m ... are theirs), `[measured]` their number on THEIR hardware (never ours), `[ours]` read in our tree or ledger, `[derived]` my reading, `[unverified]` not checked. This part adds to Part S; it does not repeat S1..S16, and where the journal gives a number Part S already used, the row here says so.

### U.1 What the repo contains

- A full radiance tree (the fork base is radiance v2.1.0, `3045979`; the author rebased 144 commits onto it on 2026-11-17) plus the gfx1100 kernel library `hcr:libr3d` (190 rows over 104 schemas; their selftest reports 196 rows, 0 failures, 16 skips; 3,181 / 3,181 cases on the 27B, 4,786 / 4,786 on Flash-Next, 1,802 / 1,802 on MiniCPM5-2B in their kbench, 0 divergence). The fork's own RDNA3 documents are in French: a work journal of 3,168 lines (one entry per work day, each measure with tree state, command, clocks), a per-kernel real-against-ideal table (835 lines), a lot tracker with a risk register R1..R58 (837 lines), an analysis report (699), a checklist, handovers of phases 2 to 7, a Flash-Next audit, and the closed M0 go / no-go memo. README states the contract: "a change is kept only if it leaves the served text bit-exact at temperature 0".
- History: 800 commits fetched through the API, 262 of them by the author (the history carries the upstream radiance commits); 343 commits are dated 2026-11-15 to 2026-11-20, the six days of the RDNA3 work (126, 60, 61, 30, 46, 20 per day; the first day includes the replayed history). No issue, no PR, no release, one branch (`main`).
- Upstream radiance PR 563 (Codeberg, open, WIP, user zihaomu, 2026-11-20): a second, independent gfx1100 port for Flash-Next TP1: reference kernels 5.9 tok/s, default 24.2, with `RAD_LOGITS_WAVE=1` 31.2 (W7900, MTP0, one sequence, 128 tokens, byte-identical text); "a step issues about a thousand small kernels"; flags in `hcr:libr3d/r3d_flags.h` where bit-identical paths are on and sum-reordering paths (`RAD_LOGITS_WAVE`, `RAD_HC_DOWN_SPLIT`) are off; PLE table kept in host memory by a plugin; no comments on the PR; the PR text says the kernels were tested only on a 48 GiB card and that 20 GB cards (RX 7900 XTX / XT / GRE) "need a larger host pool, not measured".

Journal by date (all `[journal]`, W7900 gfx1100, ROCm 10.1, host Ryzen 9 9950X with 122 GiB, PCIe 4.0 x16):
| date | entries | what was tried on gfx1100 and what came out |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-11-15 | J1, J2, W12, W13 | start from "declare fails"; IQR trellis decoder on `sudot4` + GEMV (26.4 us for 12288x2560 u5 at M=1, 744 GB/s, 86%); M0 = GO; 27B first text 28.7 tok/s; resident 16x128 tile 80 -> 303 tok/s prefill; split-K down 104 -> 67 us; WMMA int8 tile built, 25-32% slower than the `v_dot4` tile, switched off; LDS pitch 33 dwords after a 16-way bank conflict |
| 2026-11-16 | W14-W26 | Flash-Next declares (0 missing bands) and serves at 4.0-4.6 tok/s, prefill 20-42 tok/s; `hc_read` found at 1.77 ms per call (96 sites); kbench `--band-capacity` mode found 4 real bugs; host KV tier; short-tail snapshot anchor; vision; MTP NS=3 1.82x |
| 2026-11-17 | W27, W24, W21, W28, rebase | IQ8R group 256; `qsa_score` M=512 82 -> 2.2 ms (36x, lane-per-page sector amplification); `hc_read` staged in LDS 4.5x, Flash-Next decode 4.48 -> 12.73 tok/s; rebase onto radiance 2.1.0 |
| 2026-11-18 | W30-W38c | prefill attention split-KV to 128 workgroups (5-9x at q=17); vision `gemm_nt_bias` bf16 tile 8.7-11x; `hc_read` row tiling; 2x4 register tile for the trellis GEMM (27B prefill 288 -> 450 tok/s); prefill stager (1.19x at 972, decode cost found and fixed); `--tail-anchor off` (1.39x at 972); MoE narrow-tile REJECTED in service; 27B NS=3 needs a device `dflash_conv` row |
| 2026-11-19 | W40a-e, W41a-c, W42, W42g, W42m, W42b | `attn_dense` FMA / one-pass softmax tried, REJECTED (latency bound, text changes); heat swap budget schedule (-21% link bytes, -1.75% decode); the attention scratch arena was never allocated (split-KV silently off): 27B NS=3 1.74x; head-dimension split of h128 attention 11-14x bit-exact; KV page-run prefetch -6.5% decode; `tanhf` instead of double `tanh` 2.19x on one GEMM; MoE prefill walls measured, `v_dot4` rewrite REJECTED (+19-20% slower); h256 prefill attention f32 planes 1.3-1.7x at kernel, ~1% served |
| 2026-11-20 | W42 integration, W42e, W44, W43, W45, W46 | multi-stream "anomaly" was a metric error; stager R54 refuted (copies hidden); 4x4 register tile with double-buffered decode (27B prefill 450 -> 486 tok/s); 8x4 tile and 256-token block REJECTED; vision `gemm_nt_bias`: ten more variants neutral or slower |

Their progress curve on one card, for scale: Flash-Next decode 4.0-4.6 (2026-11-16) -> 12.7 (11-17) -> 11.0-12.1 at 3,252 tokens NS=0 and 19.5-19.8 NS=3 (11-19), prefill 42 tok/s -> 235 (11-17) -> 473 at 972 tokens and 616-621 at 3,252 (11-20); 27B prefill 80 -> 486 tok/s. The large steps were bug-class fixes (`hc_read`, `qsa_score`, the attention scratch arena, the h128 trunk) and the 2x4 trellis tile; from 2026-11-19 on, most entries are refused variants (W40a, W42m, W45, W46).

### U.2 Credit and licence

Apache-2.0 (LICENSE; their NOTICE: "Copyright 2026 Deadcode and the radiance contributors", with per-feature authors; libr3d is credited there to RikshaDriver and its wave-matrix rewrite, the MXFP4 GEMM to neuromanMD, and so on). The fork adds no author line of its own to NOTICE `[code]`. Apache-2.0 lets us reuse ideas and code with the licence text and the NOTICE kept. Nothing of theirs is copied here; every row is written from their text and numbers. What would need a credit (README.md section 9, a line naming the author and commit `f3e4893f`, plus the file's own notice and radiance's NOTICE) if a lead below were taken from their CODE: `hcr:libr3d/r3d_common.h` (`dot4_i8`, the both-operands-negated `sudot4` form: our `src/kernels/cuda/rdna_gr.cu` and `src/kernels/cuda/rdna_mmvq.cu` already use the same idiom, written independently; no credit for that), any `hcr:libr3d/r3d_*.hip` kernel (tiles of U5 / U7 / U8 if rewritten from theirs), `hcr:docker/llvm-vopd-pairing.patch` (an LLVM patch, LLVM's licence plus their note; S4 / U6), `hcr:cmake/RadAddPlugin.cmake` (the VOPD guard, `-ffp-contract=off`, `--no-offload-compress`). Ideas, measurements and methods (the ISA census, the ablation ladder, the exhaustive-domain proof, the sizes of negative results) need no credit but the fork's journal is cited as a source in the lead rows.

### U.3 Hardware facts they found on gfx1100 (item a), reconciled with ours

| fact | their evidence | ours / reconciliation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `v_dot4_i32_iu8` (`__builtin_amdgcn_sudot4`) EXISTS on gfx1100: signed x unsigned, so the signed product is built by setting both sign flags (`sudot4(true, w, true, a, acc, false)`) | `[code]` `hcr:libr3d/r3d_common.h` (`dot4_i8`), `hcr:libr3d/r3d_iqr_dec.h`, `hcr:libr3d/r3d_gemm_iqr_m16.hip`, `hcr:libr3d/r3d_iqr_logits.hip`, `hcr:libr3d/r3d_moe_iqr.hip` all use it; their analysis report section 3 lists `V_DOT4_I32_IU8` (opcode 22, ISA PDF 16.10) as present on gfx1100 and "compiled and emitted" | `[ours]` the same idiom in `src/kernels/cuda/rdna_gr.cu:284`, `src/kernels/cuda/rdna_mmvq.cu:72-89`, `include/strata/hip_compat/intrinsics.hpp:22-27`. CONFLICT OF PART S SETTLED: the libr3d README row "int8 dot absent on gfx11 (`s32sd4`, `s32ud4`, `__dp4a`, `madocx`)" and the comment in `hcr:libr3d/r3d_gemm_nt_q_w2a8.hip` are about BUILTIN NAMES undeclared in their AMD clang 24 for gfx1102; the instruction `v_dot4_i32_iu8` is used by the same library for its whole IQR path. The README row is wrong as worded; our kernels are right. The signed-signed `v_dot4_i32_i8` (sdot4) is the one gfx11 lacks `[derived]` |
| VOPD (dual issue) is in the gfx11 ISA (ISA PDF 16.11, p.360) and their gfx1100 builds run with it OFF | `[code]` their analysis report section 3 "VOPD: yes (ISA), not exploited: ROCm 10.1 disables the pairing"; `hcr:cmake/RadAddPlugin.cmake` adds `-mllvm=-amdgpu-enable-vopd=false` for clang >= 24 unless `RAD_HIP_VOPD_REPEATABLE`; the libr3d README row listing VOPD among features "absent on gfx11" contradicts their own report | Part S S4 stands and gets its first gfx1100 number: VOPD on one gfx1100 kernel (`attn_dense` hd72, latency-bound) was neutral, 236.1 against 235.3 ms (W31 `[journal]`). The "1.4-3.5% on prefill GEMMs" is a gfx1201 fp8 figure (their README); no gfx1100 prefill-GEMM number with VOPD ON exists in the tree because every gfx1100 build of theirs had it off. The fault is in LLVM 24's `GCNVOPDUtils.cpp` (`LoadPredsCache` over a `SmallPtrSet<SUnit*, 8>`); LLVM 22 (ROCm 7.2) is repeatable `[code]`; our clang 23 is unchecked: U6 |
| `v_dot4` is slower than 64 `v_mad_i32_i24` on a compute-bound integer tile | W42m `[journal]`: the w4nl down kernel (2,360 `v_mad_i32_i24` against 5,760 `v_bfe` / `v_ashr` / `v_lshr` extractions per tile, about 3.5 ops per MAC, 3.1 T MAC/s) rewritten with `v_dot4_i32_i8` is bit-exact and +19-20% SLOWER in prefill (972 tokens 2,491 / 2,497 ms against 2,043) and 14.03 against 14.27 tok/s decode | an end-to-end inference of "reduced rate", not an isolated rate: nobody measured the instruction. Our decode GEMVs are bandwidth-bound (MOD_PLAN.md: expert kernels about 85% of peak); our int8 `sudot4` use is in those. U4 measures the rate on our card |
| gfx11 VALU is not at the 61 TF headline in real loops: about 2.4 effective cycles per instruction | W46 `[journal]`: `gemm_nt_bias` hot loop 336 instructions for 128 MAC = 2.62 instr/MAC (128 `v_mul_f32`, 128 `v_add_f32`, 48 bf16 widenings, 12 `ds_load_2addr_b32`, 8 `s_waitcnt`), 9.2 TF/s = 39% of the issue ceiling 96 CU x 4 SIMD x 32 lanes x 2.49 GHz = 3.07e13 lane-op/s, the bare loop (2.06 lane-op/MAC) 42%: the same factor with and without the LDS reads; ablation: no LDS reads and no widening 13.94 ms against 18.4 (11.67 TF/s, the ceiling the compute path alone reaches), staging loads exposed 15-18% | the method is Part S row 1's issue-slot method with a worked example; our ceiling would be 84 x 4 x 32 x f lane-op/s (about 2.2e13 at our measured 1.98-1.99 GHz under load, T-1006) `[derived]` |
| LDS bank behaviour: pitch of a multiple of 32 dwords puts 16 rows on one bank (16-way conflict, 3% decode loss on one head kernel, 8x on a staged GEMM); odd pitches (33 dwords), +8 floats every 64 dims, 36 / 68 halfwords, an 8-element XOR swizzle fix it; a decode tile with 4 independent k chains still pays 2 LDS per MAC unless one staged weight serves two MACs | W12, W31, W32, W42b `[journal]`; their libr3d README | `[ours]` already designed in: `src/prefill/moe_fused_iq.cu:67` (`WLD = 80`, "no bank conflicts"), `src/prefill/kernels.cu:2820` (`UM_LD = LR + 8`), T-035 (bank model of the IQ codebook lookups: 5.8 -> 2.0 clocks, <= 0.5% of GPU time). No counter measures our conflicts; Part T T11 lists the counters |
| WMMA (16x16x16, wave32): operands replicate lanes 0-15 into 16-31, `k = 8*(e>>2) + 4*(lane>>4) + (e&3)`; 16-bit accumulate forms buy no throughput; TWO TRAPS: mixing f32-acc and 16-bit-acc builtins behind a runtime if/else zeroes the 16-bit path (use `if constexpr`), and a union bit-cast of the tied srcC returns the stale copy | `[code]` their libr3d README, "wave-matrix builtins" and Numerics | only f32 accumulate is used in our WMMA code (`src/prefill/kernels.cu:2800-2810`, `um_wmma` is `wmma_f32_16x16x16_bf16_w32`); hazard note for any future 16-bit-accumulate kernel (Part L iu4 leads) |
| An int8 WMMA tile lost to the `v_dot4` tile because of issue and staging, not residency | W13 `[journal]`: 12288x2560 M=512 2,801 against 2,083 us; M=4096 23,110 against 17,410; 5120x17408 M=512 7,387 against 5,683; 27B prefill 301 -> 231 tok/s with the WMMA tile; their `v_dot4` 4x4 tile later reached 5,927 us for 34816x5120 M=512, about 31 TOPS | no contradiction with our T-820 (F16 WMMA prompt-expert kernels +15.1 / +16.7 / +17.3% at 14K / 32K / 64K): a different tile, weights decoded to f16 in LDS first. It does show that a WMMA kernel that stays at 17-23 TOPS leaves most of the card's matrix rate unused; the card's iu8 dense peak is 103 TOPS on the 7900 XT (Part L, `[paper]`) |
| VRAM and cache ceilings: practical read 726-734 GB/s (84-85% of 864), cold 256 MiB copy 603-622 GB/s, a 19.7 MB GEMV reads at 744 GB/s (inside the 96 MB Infinity Cache, so not DRAM), a 15.25 GiB GEMM at 555 GB/s, split-K down 662 GB/s (76.6%) | W18, W23, W41a `[journal]` | our expert kernels sit near 85% of peak (MOD_PLAN.md, K = 1..10 experts per launch) and the card is 800 GB/s: consistent, no contradiction. Rule from them: compare a kernel with the practical ceiling and with the cold-copy number, not with 864 |
| Launch floor 3.06 us (kbench), a step of 1,589 dispatches idles 6.6% | W23, W41c `[journal]` | our empty dependent launch 2.98 us (T-809) and 395 kernels per token with gaps near 5 us (T-020, T-029): agree |
| Pinned h2d 28.5 GB/s (d2h 28.6) on PCIe 4.0 x16, 90% of 31.5 | W42m `[journal]`: `hipMemcpy` 256 MiB x 20, 188 ms | ours: Gen3 root port, probe 13.4 GB/s, promotion 115 us against 107 us (T-281, LEADS.md line "link = root port Gen3 x16 (card Gen4 x16)"). The 16.0 GT/s in Part T T.6 and in T-1006 is what the card's sysfs file shows; `tools/pancake/pcie_link.py` walks every hop and settles it without a run. No lead: the 2.1x link gap only moves their conclusions (copies hidden under compute), not our measured ones |
| `hipGetLastError` reports the first error since it was last cleared: clear before each launch check or a timing mode attributes an old fault to every geometry | `[code]` their libr3d README, "Notes on the kernels" | applies to any new probe of ours that times with launch checks (U4, U5) |
| Compile-time traps: DPP inside a loop is miscompiled on gfx1102 (they moved the FWHT to an LDS slice per wave); device `assert` is a hostcall and their queues have none (`-DNDEBUG` mandatory) | W11 `[journal]`, `hcr:cmake/RadAddPlugin.cmake` | gfx1100 is not named in the DPP note; we use DPP and `permlanex16` (`src/kernels/cuda/rdna_gr.cu`); no action, a hazard if a gfx1102 card is ever added |
| `THROTTLE_STATUS` of amd-smi reads THROTTLED even at idle on their host; they judge a measure by power and clock, and flag a kbench row `throttled (PPT0/PPT1)`; an 8-minute `--bench` throttles and its per-row times are unusable; the 4-stream points ran 10-15% below the single-stream sclk | W17, W26, W42e `[journal]` | `[ours]` `tools/pancake/abtest.py` already records sclk, mclk, edge / junction / memory temperature and watts per start; T-1006 found the drift is a cold pack read, not heat or clock (sclk 1864-2046 MHz, 174-194 W, junction 60-65 C). Their 2,287-2,496 MHz at 240-315 W (card above its 295 W TBP) against our 1.98 GHz at 174-194 W: our card is not power-limited in these runs, theirs sometimes is |

### U.4 Decode and prefill numbers, theirs against ours (item b)

Their numbers are one W7900 (gfx1100, 96 CU, 48 GiB), Flash-Next in IQR-V4 (113 GiB container: gate_up experts on a computed trellis at u3..u5, down experts on a 4-bit table `w4nl64a8h`, int8 trunk, bf16-protected experts; int8 activations; about 4 bits per weight or more), every expert computed ON THE GPU (misses are read in place over the link or staged), `expert_tiered` with a 37-38 GiB slab and a 19-24 GiB pinned pool, greedy text, one sequence. Ours is the RX 7900 XT (84 CU, 20 GB), the IQ2_XS pack (GSQ-RCO IQ2_XS, 2.3 bits per weight class), misses computed on the CPU (Ryzen 7 5700G, DDR4 33 GB/s), the daily preset with MTP spec 4, n-gram lookup, learned profile, sampling 0.1 / 0.95 / 20.
| metric | theirs `[measured]` | ours `[ours]` | what the difference is |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| decode, short context | 30.1-30.5 tok/s at ctx 92, NS=0 (W34 / W36); 36.8 at NS=3 (64 tokens in 1.74 s, W40c); PR 563's other port: 24.2 / 31.2 (MTP0, 128 tokens) | 106.9 +-7.6 short (T-822) | ours 2.9-3.5x. FORMAT plus ARCHITECTURE, not kernel: our experts are about half the bytes, 44% of expert pairs are VRAM-resident (LEADS.md "physical limits"), the CPU lane overlaps the GPU, and our rate includes MTP and lookup drafting. Their GPU-only step is latency-bound, not byte-bound (W40c: -21% link bytes bought -1.75%) |
| decode, long context | 11.8-12.1 tok/s NS=0, 19.5-19.8 NS=3 at 3,252 tokens (README, W41c); 14.3 at 972, NS=0 | edit 174.3 +-3.7 (T-822, lookup-assisted); long decode 64K case exists in the flag screen (T-1021) | their drop from 30 to 12 tok/s between ctx 92 and 3,252 is the QSA / full-attention steps and `hc_read` (hc_read 28.6 ms, attention 25.4, MoE 20.7 of 92.4 ms per token at 3,252, W40c). Ours: attention 0.74 ms of a 24.8 ms window and flat to 262K because the cell cap is 2,051 (T-017) |
| prefill | 972 tokens 2,053 ms = 473 tok/s (896 + 76 chunks); 1,409-1,488 ms with the tail anchor off = 653-690 tok/s; 3,252 tokens 5.24-5.28 s = 616-621 tok/s | long1k 801.2 +-7.8, long3k (2,934 tokens) 1,284.2 +-46.3, long64k 2,405.7 +-8.5 tok/s (T-822) | ours 1.7x at about 1K (1.2x against their tail-anchor-off), 2.1x at about 3K. Their step is compute- and link-bound on a heavier format (18.9 GiB staged per chunk, 37.7 GiB per 3,252-token prefill, hidden under compute at 28.5 GB/s); ours is half the bytes, F16 WMMA prompt-expert kernels (T-820) and a CPU share for small chunks. At 64K ours sustains about 14 TFLOP/s of model math `[derived, rough]`: about 3 GMAC per token (their W42m MoE count is 2,107 G MAC for 896 rows = 2.35 per token, plus the dense layers) x 2 x 2,406 tok/s, against the card's 103 TFLOPS f16 dense peak = 14%; at 1K 5%. So the prefill is far from the matrix peak on both machines and the gap is staging and expert IO, not instruction rate |
| 27B dense (their only dense model) | decode 15.3 tok/s (26.9 at NS=3) at 2,047 tokens; prefill 484-486 tok/s at 972 and 3,252 tokens | no dense 27B in our ledger | n/a (NOT FOR US) |
| dense prefill GEMM | trellis `v_dot4` 4x4 tile 5,927 us for 34816x5120 M=512 = about 31 TOPS; 89% of the 27B prefill is this GEMM | hipBLASLt table 1,345 -> 1,850-1,920 tok/s on an 11.7K read (T-096); no TFLOP/s per prompt kernel recorded | kernel (theirs is a software-decoded trellis) AND format; our tile rate is `[unverified]` (U5 would measure it) |
| single-kernel scale checks | `hc_read` 114 ms -> 1.93 ms at M=512 (59x, W28 -> W32); `qsa_score` 82 -> 2.2 ms; split-KV attention 5-9x at q=17; h128 trunk 11-14x | our HC read is already tiled in LDS with padded pitch (`src/prefill/kernels.cu:2796-2866`, flags `HC_UPMIX` and `PF_HCDOWN` read p64 +36..+47% and +18..+50% in the screen, both RETEST-GATE, T-1012), QSA score reads `pooled[row * D + d]` linear rows (`src/kernels/cuda/native_qsa_score.cu`) | their kernels began as straight ports with lane-per-row reads (sector amplification 16-32x) and were fixed; ours were designed tiled. A kernel difference in our favour, not a lead |

### U.5 W7900 against RX 7900 XT: what changes for transfer (item c)

| property | W7900 (theirs) | RX 7900 XT (ours) | consequence for a lead taken from them |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| compute units | 96 | 84 | their "128 workgroups = 2 per CU" split targets (W30, W40d `pick_splits`) become 168 on our card; any tile or split count tuned to 96 CU must be re-derived. Issue ceiling scales with CU x clock, 0.875 x (clock ratio) |
| VRAM | 48 GiB (slab 37.3-38.0 GiB, ring 805 MiB, KV 213-852 MiB) | 20 GB | their `max-num-seqs 1` = +650 MiB slab = -3.3% pool = -1.0% prefill (W42m) has a larger share on our 20 GB card, but our reserve test T-278 (512 MiB) was neutral; a stager that keeps the pool resident needs 18.9 GiB absent: impossible on 20 GB |
| bandwidth | 864 GB/s peak, practical 726-734 | 800 GB/s peak | scale their GB/s ceilings by 0.926 |
| host link | PCIe 4.0 x16, 28.5 GB/s | Gen3 root port, 13.4 GB/s (T-281) | their "copies are hidden under compute" (R54 refuted, W44) holds for them at 18.9 GiB per chunk in 0.71 s; on our link the same copy takes 1.5 s, so only a chunk whose compute exceeds that hides it. Their in-place read of the 76-row tail ran at the link ceiling (28.7 GB/s, 0.5 s); on ours it would take 2.1x longer, which is why our small chunks go to the CPU pool (`PREFILL_CPU_SHARE` 0.6, the preset) |
| host RAM and CPU | 122 GiB, Ryzen 9 9950X (their misses never touch the CPU) | about 57 GB, Ryzen 7 5700G, DDR4 33 GB/s (our misses are CPU work) | all of their decode-step arguments about GPU launch count and hit rate assume the GPU computes every expert; ours has a CPU lane and a 40.6% GPU wait (MOD_PLAN.md) |
| sustained clock and power | 2,287-2,496 MHz, 240-315 W, hotspot 95-100 C | 1,864-2,046 MHz, 174-194 W, junction 60-65 C (T-1006) | their TOPS and lane-op/s ceilings are about 20-25% above what our card does in the same loop |
| Infinity cache | 96 MB (their 19.7 MB GEMV sits inside it) | 80 MB | a microbenchmark under 80 MB measures cache, not DRAM: use a rotation footprint of 256 MiB as their kbench does (`--bench-flush`) |

### U.6 Their tuning knobs and variables (item d)

- Serve keys (YAML, their GUIDE 4.11): `placement: expert_tiered`, `host-pool-mib` (24,576 on W42m; 43,000 in the one-card recipe), `gpu-headroom-mib: 96` (ours: `--vram-reserve-mib`, T-278 / T-786), `kv-cache-dtype: i8`, `num-speculative-tokens: 3` (NS must match the drafter's trained block: 3 on the 27B, 7 on MiniCPM's DSpark; the other values are refused by the architecture), `max-num-seqs`, `max-num-batched-tokens` (2,048; 4,096 on one card), `checkpoint-interval` (= the step, "a prefill chunk never crosses a checkpoint"), `prefix-cache-mib`, `prefix-resume exact|nearest`, `--tail-anchor on|off` (S9), `--expert-moves N` (0 = auto, cap 1,024; the swap budget, S8), `--prefill-interleave`, `profile-ops`.
- Environment: `RADIANCE_AQL_STAMP=N` and `RADIANCE_AQL_STAMP_OUT` (a completion signal on every dispatch of every N-th pass, 1,589 dispatches per Flash-Next step; the first campaign wrote nothing and they flagged the instrument as unverified, W42b), `RADIANCE_PROFILE_EVERY`, `RAD_ATTN_BENCH=1`, `RAD_ATTN_DENSE_BENCH=1`, `RAD_HC_BENCH_M`, `RAD_IQR_BENCH=1` (timing modes inside the device tests), `RADIANCE_DEBUG_TIER_VERIFY=1` (hash after every gather and scatter), `RAD_ARCH_TRACE`, `RAD_BUILD_TRACE`, `RAD_R3D_TRACE`, `RADIANCE_TUNE` (the rad-tune cache: 36 rows, tuned `impl` / `sk` / `wv` / `nt` / `mb` / `npw` / `mrows` / `vgpr` / `acc` per machine); PR 563 adds `RAD_LOGITS_WAVE`, `RAD_HC_DOWN_SPLIT` (sum-reordering, off by default).
- Build: `RAD_GPU_TARGETS=gfx1100`, `RAD_WITH_FFMPEG`, `RAD_HIP_VOPD_REPEATABLE`, device flags `-O3 -DNDEBUG -g0 -ffp-contract=off --no-offload-compress`, `-mllvm=-amdgpu-enable-vopd=false` for clang >= 24. No `-mcumode`, no wave64, no `amdgpu-waves-per-eu` default; they only measured `waves-per-eu` 2-6 and 4 / 10 as neutral or worse (W31, W46). Wave64 and `-mcumode` were never tried on gfx1100 (the journal has no entry), so there is no negative evidence for them either.
- Nothing of the HIP-runtime queue kind (`GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES`, `HSA_*`) appears for gfx1100: their engine writes AQL packets itself (`hcr:core/device/aql.cpp`), so Part S S1 / S2 have no gfx1100 evidence from this tree.

### U.7 Already in our engine, or tested (no row)

- Heat-engine swap budget schedule (64 until 60% of the moveable bytes resident and a 4,096-selection broad pass, then 16 for 512 steps; -21% link bytes, -1.75% decode, global 16 is +6.3% at 92 tokens): this is Part S S8; nothing new in W40c beyond S8's figures except the rejected arms listed in U.9.
- Tail chunk, `--tail-anchor off`, "no gain at exactly the interval" (R46): S9; the new numbers are 1.16-1.18x at 1,536, 1.16x at 2,047, 1.01-1.02x at 2,048 (Flash-Next), 1.06-1.08x on the 27B (W38), and that a 4,096 budget with one chunk at 3,252 tokens is 19% SLOWER than 2,048 + 1,204 (6,486 against 5,453 ms, W34 / W36). Ours: `PREFILL_AUTO_MAX` 49,152 is -0.2 / -0.1 / +0.1% at long1k / long3k / long64k (T-614) and 24,576 is -0.4% on short (T-106); the preset's 32,768 stands; no contradiction in sign, ours is flat where theirs is -19%.
- Next-layer prefetch, copy resting, residency: S7 and T-528 / T-806; their W44 refutation that the second chunk is link-bound (copies hidden; removing 684 MiB of staging per pass moved nothing, 5,373 against 5,306-5,407 ms) agrees with our T-846 (PF_AHEAD -15.7%): staging more is not the lever.
- Conversation snapshots and the short-tail anchor (their R22: 24% -> 52.6% of agent tokens served): our conversation cache with recurrent-state snapshots (T-183, T-946).
- Capacity-versus-extent contract: the live row count must come from the tensors, never from the band ceiling; their `rad-kbench --band-capacity` found the bug in four kernels. Ours already has parity tests with canaries (`src/kernels/gr_parity.cpp`, `src/kernels/verify_parity.cpp`, `src/prefill/pf_cover_test.cu`); the same bug class existed in our tree once (row guard comment in `rms_norm_weighted_kernel`, `src/kernels/cuda/elementwise.cu`: a 2-row k-norm launched in 4-row blocks overwrote `b.vcur`). Residual gap: U9.
- Dispatch counters ("which kernel path a run really took, and why a fast path was declined"): `include/strata/core/dispatch_stats.hpp`, 26 sites. Gap: U3.
- Exact-bit discipline and fresh build directories: their `rsync -a` stale-object trap (a false 25% A/B) is the same one as our `tar xmf` rule.
- Attention: our QSA caps the attended cells at 2,051 (T-017, 0.74 ms of 24.8 ms at every context to 262K), so their split-KV and page-prefetch work (W30, W41c: attention 2,082 -> 1,855 us per layer, -6.5% decode at 3,252) has no equivalent ceiling here.
- Vision tower, TP2 collectives, all-reduce kernels, KV tier opaque-byte rows, MTP / DSpark rows, IQ8R group 256, MiniCPM and 27B rows: NOT FOR US (different models, no second GPU). The two-rank all-reduce and tp > 1 rows are NEEDS HARDWARE WE LACK, parked with MOD_PLAN.md section 8.

### U.8 Leads

Rule 8: each lead gets a default-off switch and a test; none is coded or run. Their gains are `[measured]` on the W7900 and mostly bit-exact; our expected gains are `[derived]`, uncertain, and under 2% end to end is below our bar (kernel gain x share >= 2% before an engine A/B). Priority is the order below.
| lead | verdict | evidence and our uncertainty | expected gain | effort / risk | default-off switch | test | where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| U1 ISA census: count, per hot loop of every gfx1100 kernel, the instructions by class (VALU f32 / f16 / i32 / f64, VOPD pairs `v_dual_*`, `v_dot2` / `v_dot4` / `v_wmma`, LDS, VMEM, SALU, waitcnt, delay) and print instructions per MAC and the issue-slot ceiling (lanes x CU x clock / instructions) next to the measured rate | APPLIES AS IS (offline, no hardware) **CODED on lead/U1-U6-U9, not run on a kernel (T-1040): `kernel_resources.py --census` / `--census-asm FILE`; fixture test only.** | their way of deciding instruction wall against bandwidth wall against link wall: `attn_dense` 437 VALU per key + 182 other = 619 slots, floor 115 ms, measured 233 ms = 70% issue (W32); w4nl down 3.5 ops per MAC at 3.1 T MAC/s (W42m); `gemm_nt_bias` 2.62 instr/MAC, 39% of 3.07e13 (W46). Ours: `tools/pancake/kernel_resources.py` gives VGPR / SGPR / scratch / spills / LDS / occupancy and ONE total instruction count plus a digest (T-859), no classes; no document of ours quotes VOPD, f64 or dot instruction counts of a hot kernel (Part T T10 asks for the audit) | none directly; it decides U2, U5, U6 and S4 and prevents a kernel project on a bandwidth-bound loop | S; risk none | `kernel_resources.py --census` (flag; without it the table and the digests stay byte-identical) | host: pytest on a stored `.s` fixture (class counts, loop found by its back-edge); PC compile-only for gfx1100 as in T-854 (ROCm 6.2 SDK), then the same census on the box with ROCm 10.0 clang 23 (compile only, no GPU) when the box is free, because the PC compiler differs | `tools/pancake/kernel_resources.py`, `tools/pancake/test_kernel_resources.py` |
| U2 Double precision in device code on gfx1100: replace f64 accumulators and f64 libm calls in named kernels by f32 (pairwise or compensated) sums, after U1 shows they sit in a top kernel | APPLIES WITH CHANGES (conditional on U1; lossy class when it changes a sum) | their W42g: the bf16 `gemm_nt_bias` epilogue called `(float)tanh((double)z)`, about 180 f64 operations per output (their README: vector f64 is soft-float at 1:64; AMD lists gfx11 FP64 at 1:32 `[unverified]`); `tanhf` instead gave fc1 38.65 -> 17.64 ms per call (2.19x), the 2,048-pixel image pass 16.5 -> 15.5 s (-6%), and was PROVEN exact for a bf16-stored result by an exhaustive run over all 65,280 finite bf16 inputs (106 codes differ in f32 bits, none after the bf16 store). Ours `[code]` (unconfirmed static analysis: nobody has shown these run on the HIP hot path): `src/kernels/cuda/elementwise.cu:70-78` (`silu_kernel`, double `exp`), `src/kernels/cuda/gdn.cu:117-147` (`gdn_l2_kernel`, `gdn_out_norm_kernel`, double sums), `src/kernels/cuda/ple.cu:75-182`, `src/kernels/cuda/qsa.cu:161-215` (key-block norm in double with `__ddiv_rn`, `__dsqrt_rn`), `src/prefill/gdn_chunk.cu`; the `DP` template of `src/kernels/cuda/fused_gr.cu` is the exact path, off by default. Our decode window is CPU-wait bound (40.6% of GPU time, MOD_PLAN.md), so a decode gain is about 0 | prefill 0-3% if one of these is in a top prompt kernel; decode about 0; unknown until U1 | S to M (swap an accumulator type, plus a parity test); risk: not bit-exact where a sum order changes, so the quality gate and token forcing apply | `STRATA_REDUCE_F32=1` (default off; classed Speed in `src/core/conversation_disk_env.def`) | host: U1 census first (zero f64 in the top kernels closes the lead); parity of each changed kernel against its double form on random and worst-case rows; box: kernel time, then the prompt A/B (long1k, long3k) and the quality gate | files listed in the evidence column |
