### U.9 Negative evidence from their journal (item e): record, do not repeat

All `[journal]`, gfx1100, bit-exact variants unless said; "refused" means rejected on measurement.
1. int8 WMMA tile for the trellis prefill GEMM: 25-32% slower than the `v_dot4` resident tile at every shape; 27B prefill 301 -> 231 tok/s (W13).
2. Register tile 8x4 per lane: unroll 4 is 1.65x slower, unroll 8 still 1.04-1.08x slower at the kernel and -6..-8% served; 256-token block on the 4x4 tile: 1.05-1.06x slower (8 waves leave 16 resident waves per CU against 20); loop inversion no change; do not reopen beyond 4x4 / 128 tokens (R57, W45).
3. `v_dot4_i32_i8` for the w4nl MoE down tile: bit-exact, +19-20% slower prefill, decode 14.03 against 14.27 (W42m).
4. Narrow MoE run-map tiles (16 / 32 rows): kbench 1.27x at M=128, +8.5% slower in service at M=1204 (about 23.5 rows per expert: the weight unit is decoded twice); the change also stopped the engine because a declaration was sized for the band's tile, not the narrowest possible tile (W32, R39).
5. Staging the 76-row tail (`kStagerMinRows` 256 -> 64): kernels faster (MoE 1,175 -> 700 ms), copy exposed, wall unchanged (2,047.6 against 2,043 ms); a staged 82-token chunk costs more than it returns (0.81 against 0.72 s) (W34, W42m).
6. Merging the tail chunk by raising `kShortTailMin`, or one 4,096 chunk at 3,252 tokens: +19% slower (6,486 against 5,453 ms) (W36).
7. Heat swap budgets: global 16 +6.3% worse at 92 tokens (hit 75.2 -> 70.6%), global 48 +0.85%, expert slots 2 -> 4 -1.4% (noise), no heat / profile at all (`--deterministic`) 6,746 against 5,910 ms (W40c).
8. `attn_dense` hd72: FMA 3.3% slower (a 72-op dependent chain, latency bound), lane = key +23%, `kStage` 64 9.9x slower (36 KiB LDS destroys occupancy), `__launch_bounds__(128,3)` 3.7x slower, waves-per-EU 2 / 3 / 5 / 6, 256 threads, VOPD: all neutral; one-pass softmax 1.46x faster but changes the served text, refused (W31, W32, W40a).
9. `hc_read`: pre-decoded f32 planes lose 26-30% at M=128-512 (4x the weight bytes re-read from L2); register prefetch of codes (68 -> 83 VGPR, 2 workgroups per CU) neutral; P2Q4 / P4Q2 tiles save at most 2% of the instructions for a drop to two blocks per CU; computing the norm once per row +8% net, under their 1.15x bar; WMMA and `v_dot2` forbidden by the accumulation order (W40b, W33).
10. Vision `gemm_nt_bias`, ten variants (`kKT` 32 / 128, register prefetch even with `sched_barrier` asm, 8x8 tile, `ldg_u32` staging, `waves-per-eu` 4 / 10): neutral or 1.25-1.35x slower; the 64 x 64 tile at 2.62 instr/MAC is a local optimum (W46).
11. `qsa_score` with four blocks per thread: 2x slower (more distinct pages per instruction) (W24).
12. Decode attention with f32 planes in LDS: +31% at 8K (the decode band has two workgroups per CU and runs out of LDS bandwidth first), while the prefill band gains 1.3-1.7x from them (W42b).
13. Batching four KV pages through an indexed array: spilled to local memory, slower; batching the down / attention dots neutral (W41c).
14. Decode GEMV: coalesced continuation, `__syncwarp` in the fold, simplified trellis MAD, regrouped slices all neutral (W41a); distributing the trellis decode of a unit over four waves 1.04x at M=1626 and 0.997x at M=512 (W33).
15. Split-KV for the causal h256 trunk in service: 1.73x faster, text changes by one bf16 ulp, refused by their contract (W40d); the h128 trunk was fixed instead by splitting the OUTPUT dimension (11-14x, bit-exact, R51): never split a reduction over keys when order is part of the contract.
16. Prefill stager and gated MLP: pre-staging between chunks is inert by design (the half-ring rule needs 420.4 MiB against a 402.8 MiB half ring, R56); an occupancy lot of the gated kernel delivered nothing (W13).
17. Measurement traps that cost them days: an incremental rebuild after `rsync -a` showed a false 25% loss (W13); `RADIANCE_AQL_STAMP` wrote nothing in a whole campaign (W42b); a kbench run of 8 minutes throttles and its per-row times are void (entry of 2026-11-16, "Flash-Next servi"); an aggregate "52-67 tok/s" for four streams was a short-context number and a "wall" rate compared with a "window" rate (W40e, W42e).

### U.10 Top 5 recommended leads (cheapest first; the first two need no box)

1. U1 (ISA census): offline on this PC, an afternoon, and it decides U2, U5, U6 and S4: it tells which hot kernels contain f64, how many VOPD pairs exist, and which loops are instruction-bound before any GPU time is spent.
2. U3 (served-path census): the cheapest protection of every flag verdict; extend 26 existing counter sites to the prompt and RDNA kernels and let the harness mark INERT arms (22 of 76 screened flags are INCONCLUSIVE; at least four past results were inert arms, T-044, T-048, T-803, T-984).
3. U4 (instruction-rate probe): two minutes of box time; settles the sudot4 / dot4 / VALU rates that their journal infers and their README contradicts, and says whether `rdna_kq` and the expert decode kernels can still be instruction-bound.
4. U5 (ablation ladder of the F16 prompt-expert kernel): the only lead here with a possible prompt gain above 5%; one small box slot, bench target only.
5. U2 (double precision audit): free after U1, and on their evidence the largest single kernel factor in the tree (2.19x on one GEMM); our reach is `[unverified]` (the candidate kernels may not run on the HIP path), so it is rank 5 until the census names a top kernel.

### U.11 What needs the box (the box is busy with the flag screen and was not used: no ssh, no build, no test; this PC has a GTX 1660 Ti and no gfx1100 GPU)

Host-only here: U1 (fixture test, and compile-only gfx1100 with the PC's ROCm 6.2 SDK as in T-854), the report parser and INERT rule of U3, the helper of U9, the repeat loop of U6 with a fake compiler. Box, compile only (no GPU): U1 with clang 23, U6. Box, GPU (queue behind the flag screen): U4 (about 2 minutes, GPU idle), U5 (one micro-benchmark session), U7 and U8 (kernel microbench and parity binaries only; engine screens not justified), U9 (one start per width), the U3 start with the report on, and U2's kernel and prompt A/B only if U1 names a kernel.

### U.12 Not verified (`[unverified]`)

Whether any f64 kernel of U2 runs on our HIP hot path; the instruction rates of `v_dot4_i32_iu8` and of VALU on our card (U4); whether clang 23 shares LLVM 24's non-repeatable VOPD pairing; the time split of our F16 prompt-expert kernel between staging, decode and WMMA, and its TFLOP/s; whether our QSA prompt-side indexer reads block keys coalesced (the decode score kernel reads linear `pooled` rows, `[code]`); FP64 rate on gfx11 (their README says 1:64, I recall 1:32); Infinity cache sizes (96 MB and 80 MB are AMD's specification, not in either tree); the cause of the 16.0 GT/s sysfs reading against a Gen3 root port; every number in the tables is the fork author's or a poster's on their hardware; I read the journal in full, the libr3d README, the analysis report sections 1-5, the final handover, the final tables, the risk register lines and the build files, but not the 835-line real-against-ideal table in full, the checklist, the Flash-Next audit, `spec.md`, the per-kernel headers beyond the ones cited, or the docs folder of the fork.
